\displaystyle 2q_{\mu_{1}}J_{2}^{\left(2\right)\mu_{12}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left(q^{2}+m_{1}^{2}-m_{2}^{2}\right)J_{2}^{\left(2\right)\mu_{2}}-\frac{i}{4\pi}q^{\mu_{2}}\log\left(m_{2}^{2}/\lambda^{2}\right)

(3.67)

\displaystyle g_{\mu_{12}}J_{2}^{\left(2\right)\mu_{12}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{i}{4\pi}+m_{1}^{2}J_{2}^{\left(2\right)}\right)-\frac{i}{4\pi}\log\left(m_{2}^{2}/\lambda^{2}\right)

(3.68)

\displaystyle 2q_{\mu_{1}}J_{2}^{\left(2\right)\mu_{1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\left(q^{2}+m_{1}^{2}-m_{2}^{2}\right)J_{2}^{\left(2\right)}+\frac{i}{4\pi}\log\left(m_{2}^{2}/m_{1}^{2}\right)

(3.69)

\displaystyle q^{2}(2J_{2\mu_{2}}^{\left(2\right)}+q_{\mu_{2}}J_{2}^{\left(2\right)})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-q_{\mu_{2}}\left(m_{1}^{2}-m_{2}^{2}\right)J_{2}^{\left(2\right)}-\frac{i}{4\pi}q_{\mu_{2}}\log\left(m_{1}^{2}/m_{2}^{2}\right).

(3.70)

In Chapter (
7
), in addition to the functions introduced above,
it is necessary to the single mass of 3rd-rank integral, obtained by
applying the identity with
N=1
:

\bar{J}_{2\mu_{123}}^{\left(2\right)}=J_{2\mu_{123}}^{\left(2\right)}-\frac{1}{4}P^{\nu_{1}}W_{3\mu_{123}\nu_{1}}+\frac{1}{4}\left(P-q\right)_{(\mu_{1}}\Delta_{2\mu_{23})}-\frac{1}{4}q_{(\mu_{1}}g_{\mu_{23})}I_{\log}

(3.71)

J_{2\mu_{123}}^{\left(2\right)}=-\frac{i}{4\pi}\left[-\frac{1}{2}q_{(\mu_{1}}g_{\mu_{23})}Z_{1}^{\left(0\right)}+q_{\mu_{123}}Z_{3}^{\left(-1\right)}\right].

(3.72)

And 4th-rank integral, using
N=2
in (
3.4
):

\displaystyle\bar{J}_{2\mu_{12}\alpha_{12}}^{\left(2\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle J_{2\mu_{12}\alpha_{12}}^{\left(2\right)}+\frac{1}{4}W_{2\mu_{12}\alpha_{12}}+\frac{1}{4}g_{(\mu_{12}\alpha_{12})}I_{\mathrm{quad}}

(3.73)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{24}\left[q^{2}g_{(\mu_{12}\alpha_{12})}-4q_{(\mu_{12}}g_{\alpha_{12})}\right]I_{\log}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{48}\left(3P^{\nu_{12}}+q^{\nu_{12}}\right)W_{4\mu_{12}\alpha_{12}\nu_{12}}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{16}\left(P^{2}+q^{2}\right)W_{3\mu_{12}\alpha_{12}}-\frac{1}{8}P^{\nu_{1}}\left(P-q\right)_{(\mu_{1}}W_{3\mu_{2}\alpha_{12})\nu_{1}}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{8}\left[\left(P-q\right)_{\mu_{1}}\left(P-q\right)_{(\alpha_{1}}\Delta_{2\alpha_{2})\mu_{2}}+\left(P-q\right)_{\mu_{2}}\left(P-q\right)_{(\alpha_{1}}\Delta_{2\alpha_{2})\mu_{1}}\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{8}\left(P-q\right)_{\alpha_{1}}\left(P-q\right)_{\alpha_{2}}\Delta_{2\mu_{12}}+\frac{1}{8}\left(P-q\right)_{\mu_{1}}\left(P-q\right)_{\mu_{2}}\Delta_{2\alpha_{12}}

J_{2\mu_{12}\alpha_{12}}^{\left(2\right)}=\frac{i}{4\pi}\left\{\frac{1}{4}g_{(\mu_{12}}g_{\alpha_{12})}\left[Z_{0}^{\left(1\right)}-m^{2}\right]-\frac{1}{2}g_{(\mu_{12}}q_{\alpha_{12})}Z_{2}^{\left(0\right)}+q_{\mu_{12}}q_{\alpha_{12}}Z_{4}^{\left(-1\right)}\right\}.

(3.74)

We use index condensation notation for momentum,
q_{\mu_{1}}...q_{\mu_{n}}=q_{\mu_{1}...\mu_{n}},
as well as for metric
g_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}=g_{\mu_{12}}.
Remembering that
q_{(\mu_{12}}g_{\alpha_{12})}
is
the symmetric combination.

Using the reduction of the last section, we derive the identities

\displaystyle 2q^{\mu_{1}}J_{2\mu_{123}}^{\left(2\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-q^{2}J_{2\mu_{2}\mu_{3}}^{\left(2\right)}

(3.75)

\displaystyle 2q^{\mu_{1}}J_{2\mu_{1234}}^{\left(2\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-q^{2}J_{2\mu_{234}}^{\left(2\right)}.

(3.76)

And the contraction with the metric tensor given by

\displaystyle 2g_{\mu_{12}}J_{2}^{\left(2\right)\mu_{123}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2m^{2}J_{2}^{\left(2\right)\mu_{3}}-\frac{i}{4\pi}q^{\mu_{3}}

(3.77)

\displaystyle 2g_{\mu_{12}}J_{2}^{\left(2\right)\mu_{1234}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2m^{2}J_{2}^{\left(2\right)\mu_{34}}+\frac{i}{4\pi}\frac{1}{6}\left[3q^{\mu_{34}}-\theta^{\mu_{34}}\left(q\right)\right].

(3.78)

3.3.2
Four Dimensions

As to the four-dimensional integral, we have the following power counting

\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\omega(J_{3}^{\left(4\right)})=-2\\
\omega(J_{3}^{\left(4\right)\mu_{1}})=-1\\
\omega(J_{3}^{\left(4\right)\mu_{12}})=0\end{array}\right.;\quad\left\{\begin{array}[]{c}\omega(J_{2}^{\left(4\right)})=0\\
\omega(J_{2}^{\left(4\right)\mu_{1}})=1\end{array}\right.;

(3.79)

The scalar and vector three-point functions are finite:
\bar{J}_{3}^{\left(4\right)}=J_{3}^{\left(4\right)}
and
\bar{J}_{3}^{\left(4\right)\mu_{1}}=J_{3}^{\left(4\right)\mu_{1}}
. We compute the case with the
highest power counting to illustrate some features of our treatment. The
four-dimensional vector two-point integral,

\bar{J}_{2}^{\left(4\right)\mu_{1}}=\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{4}k}{\left(2\pi\right)^{4}}\frac{K_{i}^{\mu_{1}}}{D_{ij}}

(3.80)

has linear power counting, which requires using the identity (
3.4
) with

N=1,
as (
3.58
). Its replacement allows rewriting the integrand

\displaystyle\frac{K_{i}^{\mu_{1}}}{D_{ij}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{K_{i}^{\mu_{1}}}{D_{\lambda}^{2}}-\frac{\left(A_{i}+A_{j}\right)K_{i}^{\mu_{1}}}{D_{\lambda}^{3}}

(3.81)

\displaystyle+\left[\frac{A_{i}A_{j}}{D_{\lambda}^{4}}+\frac{A_{i}^{2}}{D_{\lambda}^{3}D_{i}}+\frac{A_{j}^{2}}{D_{\lambda}^{3}D_{j}}-\frac{A_{i}A_{j}^{2}}{D_{\lambda}^{4}D_{j}}-\frac{A_{j}A_{i}^{2}}{D_{\lambda}^{4}D_{i}}+\frac{A_{i}^{2}A_{j}^{2}}{D_{\lambda}^{4}D_{ij}}\right]K_{i}^{\mu_{1}}.

After applying the integration sign, we gather the purely divergent
integrals and integrate the remaining finite integrals.

This result exhibits all elements presented before. We organize the local
divergences through surface terms and irreducible scalars,

\bar{J}_{2}^{\left(4\right)\mu_{1}}=J_{2}^{\left(4\right)\mu_{1}}\left(p_{ji}\right)-\frac{1}{2}[P_{ji}^{\nu_{1}}\Delta_{3\nu_{1}}^{\left(4\right)\mu_{1}}+p_{ji}^{\mu_{1}}I_{\log}^{\left(4\right)}],

(3.82)

while integrating the finite part without restrictions,

\ J_{2}^{\left(4\right)\mu_{1}}\left(p_{ji}\right)=\frac{i}{\left(4\pi\right)^{2}}p_{ji}^{\mu_{1}}Z_{1}^{\left(0\right)}(p_{ij}^{2},m^{2}),

(3.83)

where
p_{ij}=k_{i}-k_{j}
and
P_{ij}=k_{i}+k_{j}
(
2.8
-
2.10
).
For completeness, the scalar integral,

\bar{J}_{2}^{\left(4\right)}=I_{\log}^{\left(4\right)}+J_{2}^{\left(4\right)}\left(p_{ji}\right).

(3.84)

Following our organization, its finite part is given by

J_{2}^{\left(4\right)}\left(p_{ij}\right)=-\frac{i}{\left(4\pi\right)^{2}}Z_{0}^{\left(0\right)}(p_{ij}^{2},m^{2}).

Three-Point:
We need scalar, vector, and tensor integrals.

\displaystyle J_{3}^{\left(4\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\left(4\pi\right)^{-2}[Z_{00}^{\left(-1\right)}\left(p,q\right)]

(3.85)

\displaystyle J_{3\mu_{1}}^{\left(4\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\left(4\pi\right)^{-2}[-p_{\mu_{1}}Z_{10}^{\left(-1\right)}-q_{\mu_{1}}Z_{01}^{\left(-1\right)}]

(3.86)

\displaystyle J_{3\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}^{\left(4\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\left(4\pi\right)^{-2}\left[p_{\mu_{12}}Z_{20}^{\left(-1\right)}+q_{\mu_{12}}Z_{02}^{\left(-1\right)}+p_{(\mu_{1}}q_{\mu_{2})}Z_{11}^{\left(-1\right)}-\frac{1}{2}g_{\mu_{12}}Z_{00}^{\left(0\right)}\right]

(3.87)

\bar{J}_{3\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}^{\left(4\right)}=J_{3\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}^{\left(4\right)}+\frac{1}{4}(\Delta_{3\mu_{12}}^{\left(4\right)}+g_{\mu_{12}}I_{\log}^{\left(4\right)}).

(3.88)

Of these, only the tensor integral is divergent, where we used
N=0
, in (
3.4
). It is worth mentioning that the arguments
p
and
q
are only
general variables that tag the entries of the functions; they must be
carefully substituted for the ones that appear in a particular part of the
investigation. In four dimensions, we will adopt
p=p_{21}
and
q=p_{31}
.

Reductions
\mathbf{4D}
:
The three points obey the
reductions of the previous section as the two-point functions. Therefore it
is possible to show that the tensors
J
satisfy

\displaystyle 2p^{\mu_{1}}J_{3\mu_{1}}^{\left(4\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-p^{2}J_{3}^{\left(4\right)}+[J_{2}^{\left(4\right)}\left(q\right)-J_{2}^{\left(4\right)}\left(q-p\right)]

(3.89)

\displaystyle 2q^{\mu_{1}}J_{3\mu_{1}}^{\left(4\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-q^{2}J_{3}^{\left(4\right)}+[J_{2}^{\left(4\right)}\left(p\right)-J_{2}^{\left(4\right)}\left(q-p\right)].

(3.90)

And for the tensor integrals

\displaystyle 2p^{\mu_{1}}J_{3\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}^{\left(4\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-p^{2}J_{3\mu_{2}}^{\left(4\right)}+[J_{2\mu_{2}}^{\left(4\right)}\left(q\right)+J_{2\mu_{2}}^{\left(4\right)}\left(q-p\right)+q_{\mu_{2}}J_{2}^{\left(4\right)}\left(q-p\right)]

(3.91)

\displaystyle 2q^{\mu_{1}}J_{3\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}^{\left(4\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-q^{2}J_{3\mu_{2}}^{\left(4\right)}+[J_{2\mu_{2}}^{\left(4\right)}\left(p\right)+J_{2\mu_{2}}^{\left(4\right)}\left(q-p\right)+q_{\mu_{2}}J_{2}^{\left(4\right)}\left(q-p\right)].

(3.92)

In addition to the trace contraction

g^{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}J_{3\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}^{\left(4\right)}=m^{2}J_{3}^{\left(4\right)}+\frac{i}{2\left(4\pi\right)^{2}}+J_{2}^{\left(4\right)}\left(q-p\right).

(3.93)

In sections where a specific dimension is handled, we drop the super-index
in
J^{\left(d\right)}
integrals.

We will also need the reductions of the
Z
-functions for the case of
different masses

\displaystyle Z_{0}^{\left(1\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(-m_{2}^{2}\right)\left[\log\frac{m_{2}^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}-1\right]+2p^{2}Z_{2}^{\left(0\right)}-\left(p^{2}+m_{1}^{2}-m_{2}^{2}\right)Z_{1}^{\left(0\right)}

(3.94)

\displaystyle 2q^{2}Z_{1}^{\left(0\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(q^{2}+m_{1}^{2}-m_{2}^{2}\right)Z_{0}^{\left(0\right)}+m_{2}^{2}\log\frac{m_{2}^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}-m_{1}^{2}\log\frac{m_{1}^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}+\left(m_{1}^{2}-m_{2}^{2}\right)

(3.95)

\displaystyle q^{2}Z_{n+2}^{\left(0\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\left(n+2\right)}{\left(n+3\right)}\left(q^{2}+m_{1}^{2}-m_{2}^{2}\right)Z_{n+1}^{\left(0\right)}-\frac{\left(n+1\right)}{\left(n+3\right)}m_{1}^{2}Z_{n}^{\left(0\right)}

(3.97)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\left(n+3\right)}m_{2}^{2}\log\frac{m_{2}^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}-\frac{1}{\left(n+3\right)\left(n+2\right)}\left[\frac{\left(n+1\right)}{\left(n+3\right)}q^{2}+\left(m_{2}^{2}-m_{1}^{2}\right)\right].

All the results of this Session also will be used to determine under what
conditions the Einstein and Weyl anomalies manifest themselves in the
gravitational amplitudes. However, in the following two Chapters, we will
verify the explicit form of the odd two-dimensional and four-dimensional
abelian chiral amplitudes. After doing this, we will extend the results to
the two-dimensional gravitational case.

Chapter 4
Two-Dimensional
AV
-
VA
Functions

In this section, we compute amplitudes of two Lorentz indices
to establish the connection between linearity, symmetries, and low-energy
implications, which materialize through Relations Among Green Functions
(RAGFs) and Ward Identities (WIs). It is also defined what we mean by
uniqueness, exploring examples that evoke this concept. Since the involved
amplitudes exhibit logarithmic power counting, they depend only on the
difference between routings and not on the arbitrary sums; then, we adopt
q=p_{21}=k_{2}-k_{1}
.

Our first step, therefore, is to clarify the mentioned connection. After
introducing the model
2
, we showed how to establish identities
among the amplitudes integrands (
2.14
)-(
2.17
). The integration
should produce RAGFs for the vector and axial vertexes

\displaystyle q^{\mu_{2}}T_{\mu_{12}}^{AV}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{\mu_{1}}^{A}\left(1\right)-T_{\mu_{1}}^{A}\left(2\right)

(4.1)

\displaystyle q^{\mu_{1}}T_{\mu_{12}}^{VA}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{\mu_{2}}^{A}\left(1\right)-T_{\mu_{2}}^{A}\left(2\right).

(4.2)

\displaystyle q^{\mu_{1}}T_{\mu_{12}}^{AV}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{\mu_{2}}^{A}\left(1\right)-T_{\mu_{2}}^{A}\left(2\right)-2mT_{\mu_{2}}^{PV}

(4.3)

\displaystyle q^{\mu_{2}}T_{\mu_{12}}^{VA}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{\mu_{1}}^{A}\left(1\right)-T_{\mu_{1}}^{A}\left(2\right)+2mT_{\mu_{1}}^{VP}.

(4.4)

These contractions are direct implications of the integral linearity, and
conditions to their validity are the subject of the first subsection.
Meanwhile, WIs require vanishing the axial one-point functions above. That
occurs because the formal current-conservation equations require it (
2.21
and
2.20
).

Moreover, if these symmetry constraints are valid, the general structure of
these amplitudes as odd tensors implies kinematic properties to the scalar
invariants
F_{i}
as,

T_{\mu_{12}}^{AV}=\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}F_{1}+\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}\nu}q^{\nu}q_{\mu_{2}}F_{2}+\varepsilon_{\mu_{2}\nu}q^{\nu}q_{\mu_{1}}F_{3}.

(4.5)

Contracting with the external momenta in the respective indexes yields

\displaystyle q^{\mu_{2}}T_{\mu_{12}}^{AV}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}\nu}q^{\nu}(q^{2}F_{2}+F_{1}),

(4.6)

\displaystyle q^{\mu_{1}}T_{\mu_{12}}^{AV}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\mu_{2}\nu}q^{\nu}(q^{2}F_{3}-F_{1}).

(4.7)

The vector conservation in the first equation implies
F_{1}=-q^{2}F_{2},

whose replacement in the second equation produces

q^{\mu_{1}}T_{\mu_{12}}^{AV}=\varepsilon_{\mu_{2}\nu}q^{\nu}q^{2}(F_{3}+F_{2}).

(4.8)

Hence, if invariants do not have poles in
q^{2}=0
, we have a low-energy
implication for axial contraction. If axial WI is satisfied, this
implication falls on the
PV
amplitude

\left.q^{\mu_{1}}T_{\mu_{12}}^{AV}\right|_{q^{2}=0}=0=\left.-2mT_{\mu_{2}}^{PV}\right|_{q^{2}=0}=:\varepsilon_{\mu_{2}\nu}q^{\nu}\Omega^{PV}(q^{2}=0),

(4.9)

being
\Omega^{PV}
is the form factor associated with
PV
. The deduction
of this last behavior requires the validity of both WIs, so it has the same
status as a symmetry property. The reciprocal form of this statement appears
by exchanging the order of the arguments. If the axial WI is selected first,
it implies
F_{1}=q^{2}F_{3}-\Omega^{PV}
in (
4.7
). Its
replacement in the vector contraction (
4.6
) gives the low-energy
implication for the contraction with the index of the vector current

\left.q^{\mu_{2}}T_{\mu_{12}}^{AV}\right|_{q^{2}=0}=-\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}\nu}q^{\nu}\Omega^{PV}(q^{2}=0).

(4.10)

With this scenario in hand, our objective is their analysis in the light of
explicit integration (
2.11
). From definition (
2.9
), the general
integrand of two-point amplitudes is

\displaystyle t^{\Gamma_{1}\Gamma_{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle K_{12}^{\nu_{12}}\text{{tr}}[\Gamma_{1}\gamma_{\nu_{1}}\Gamma_{2}\gamma_{\nu_{2}}]\frac{1}{D_{12}}+m^{2}\text{{tr}}[\Gamma_{1}\Gamma_{2}]\frac{1}{D_{12}},

(4.11)

\displaystyle+mK_{1}^{\nu_{1}}\text{{tr}}[\Gamma_{1}\gamma_{\nu_{1}}\Gamma_{2}]\frac{1}{D_{12}}+mK_{2}^{\nu_{1}}\text{{tr}}[\Gamma_{1}\Gamma_{2}\gamma_{\nu_{1}}]\frac{1}{D_{12}}.

Specific versions emerge after choosing the vertices and keeping the
non-zero traces:

\displaystyle t_{\mu_{12}}^{AV}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle K_{12}^{\nu_{12}}\mathrm{tr}(\gamma_{\ast}\gamma_{\mu_{1}\nu_{1}\mu_{2}\nu_{2}})\frac{1}{D_{12}}+m^{2}\mathrm{tr}(\gamma_{\ast}\gamma_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}})\frac{1}{D_{12}},

(4.12)

\displaystyle t_{\mu_{12}}^{VA}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle K_{12}^{\nu_{12}}\mathrm{tr}(\gamma_{\ast}\gamma_{\mu_{1}\nu_{1}\mu_{2}\nu_{2}})\frac{1}{D_{12}}-m^{2}\mathrm{tr}(\gamma_{\ast}\gamma_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}})\frac{1}{D_{12}}.

(4.13)

As the trace of four gamma matrices is a linear combination of the metric
and the Levi-Civita tensor, various expressions emerge through substitutions
involving the following versions of the identity (
2.5
):

\displaystyle 2\gamma_{\ast}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\nu_{12}}\gamma^{\nu_{12}};

(4.14)

\displaystyle\gamma_{\ast}\gamma_{\mu_{1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}\nu_{1}}\gamma^{\nu_{1}};

(4.15)

\displaystyle\gamma_{\ast}\gamma_{\left[\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\right]}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}.

(4.16)

They lead to expressions that are not automatically equal after integration.
To unfold this rationale, let us apply the chiral matrix definition in form
2\gamma_{\ast}=\varepsilon^{ef}\gamma_{ef}
to write

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}(\gamma_{\ast}\gamma_{abcd})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon^{ef}\mathrm{tr}(\gamma_{efabcd})

(4.17)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2[-g_{ab}\varepsilon_{cd}+g_{ac}\varepsilon_{bd}-g_{ad}\varepsilon_{bc}-g_{bc}\varepsilon_{ad}+g_{bd}\varepsilon_{ac}-g_{cd}\varepsilon_{ab}].

(4.18)

We explore two equivalent sorting of indices
\left(a,b,c,d\right)=\left(\mu_{1},\nu_{1},\mu_{2},\nu_{2}\right)
and
\left(a,b,c,d\right)=\left(\mu_{2},\nu_{2},\mu_{1},\nu_{1}\right)
, corresponding to the
substitution of the chiral matrix definition around the first and second
vertices. The traces differ by signs of terms but are equivalent. To study
them, we perform the contractions with
K_{12}^{\nu_{12}}=K_{1}^{\nu_{1}}K_{2}^{\nu_{2}}
and write the equations

\displaystyle K_{12}^{\nu_{12}}\mathrm{tr}(\gamma_{\ast}\gamma_{\mu_{1}}\gamma_{\nu_{1}}\gamma_{\mu_{2}}\gamma_{\nu_{2}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}\nu_{1}}\left(K_{1\mu_{2}}K_{2}^{\nu_{1}}+K_{2\mu_{2}}K_{1}^{\nu_{1}}\right)-2\varepsilon_{\mu_{2}\nu_{1}}\left(K_{1\mu_{1}}K_{2}^{\nu_{1}}-K_{2\mu_{1}}K_{1}^{\nu_{1}}\right)

(4.19)

\displaystyle+2\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}\left(K_{1}\cdot K_{2}\right)+2g_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}\varepsilon_{\nu_{1}\nu_{2}}K_{12}^{\nu_{12}},

\displaystyle K_{12}^{\nu_{12}}\mathrm{tr}(\gamma_{\ast}\gamma_{\mu_{2}}\gamma_{\nu_{2}}\gamma_{\mu_{1}}\gamma_{\nu_{1}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+2\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}\nu_{1}}\left(K_{1\mu_{2}}K_{2}^{\nu_{1}}-K_{2\mu_{2}}K_{1}^{\nu_{1}}\right)-2\varepsilon_{\mu_{2}\nu_{1}}\left(K_{1\mu_{1}}K_{2}^{\nu_{1}}+K_{2\mu_{1}}K_{1}^{\nu_{1}}\right)

(4.20)

\displaystyle-2\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}\left(K_{1}\cdot K_{2}\right)-2g_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}\varepsilon_{\nu_{1}\nu_{2}}K_{12}^{\nu_{12}}.

The general form (
4.11
) shows that combining the bilinears with mass
terms associated with
\mathrm{tr}(\gamma_{\ast}\gamma_{\mu_{12}})=-2\varepsilon_{\mu_{12}}
leads to scalar two-point amplitudes
identified as

\displaystyle t^{PP}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle q^{2}\frac{1}{D_{12}}-\frac{1}{D_{1}}-\frac{1}{D_{2}},

(4.21)

\displaystyle t^{SS}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(4m^{2}-q^{2})\frac{1}{D_{12}}+\frac{1}{D_{1}}+\frac{1}{D_{2}}.

(4.22)

The following reduction was used for these integrands

S_{ij}=K_{i}\cdot K_{j}-m^{2}=\frac{1}{2}(D_{i}+D_{j}-p_{ij}^{2}).

(4.23)

It is possible to express all other contributions in terms of the same
object, a standard tensor present similarly in all explored dimensions

t_{\mu_{2}}^{\left(s_{1}\right)\nu_{1}}=\left(K_{1\mu_{2}}K_{2}^{\nu_{1}}+s_{1}K_{2\mu_{2}}K_{1}^{\nu_{1}}\right)\frac{1}{D_{12}}.

(4.24)

where the
s_{1}=

\pm
. The tensors that arise from the expression above
are given by

\displaystyle t_{\mu_{12}}^{\left(+\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\frac{K_{1\mu_{1}}K_{1\mu_{2}}}{D_{12}}+q_{(\mu_{1}}K_{1\mu_{2})}\frac{1}{D_{12}}

(4.25)

\displaystyle t_{\mu_{12}}^{\left(-\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle q_{[\mu_{2}}K_{1\mu_{1}]}\frac{1}{D_{12}}.

(4.26)

Nevertheless, anticipating a connection with higher dimensions, we opt to
write the last term as a pseudo-scalar function

t^{SP}=-t^{PS}=\varepsilon_{\nu_{1}\nu_{2}}t^{\left(-\right)\nu_{1}\nu_{2}}=2\frac{\varepsilon_{\nu_{1}\nu_{2}}K_{1}^{\nu_{1}}K_{2}^{\nu_{2}}}{D_{12}}

using
\varepsilon_{\nu_{1}\nu_{2}}K_{1}^{\nu_{1}}K_{2}^{\nu_{2}}=\varepsilon_{\nu_{1}\nu_{2}}p_{21}^{\nu_{2}}K_{1}^{\nu_{1}}
and
then the definition of the vector integral for equal masses, proportional to

p_{21}^{\nu_{1}}Z_{1},
results in

T^{SP}=2\varepsilon_{\nu_{1}\nu_{2}}q^{\nu_{2}}J_{2}^{\nu_{1}}=0.

Therefore, given both versions for the four-matrix trace, we have the
corresponding versions for the
AV
amplitude

\displaystyle(t_{\mu_{12}}^{AV})_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}\nu_{1}}t_{\mu_{2}}^{\left(+\right)\nu_{1}}-\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}t^{PP}-2\varepsilon_{\mu_{2}\nu_{1}}t_{\mu_{1}}^{\left(-\right)\nu_{1}}+2g_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}t^{SP},

(4.27)

\displaystyle(t_{\mu_{12}}^{AV})_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\varepsilon_{\mu_{2}\nu_{1}}t_{\mu_{1}}^{\left(+\right)\nu_{1}}-\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}t^{SS}+2\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}\nu_{1}}t_{\mu_{2}}^{\left(-\right)\nu_{1}}-2g_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}t^{SP}.

(4.28)

The same happens to the
VA
amplitude

\displaystyle(t_{\mu_{12}}^{VA})_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}\nu_{1}}t_{\mu_{2}}^{\left(+\right)\nu_{1}}+\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}t^{SS}-2\varepsilon_{\mu_{2}\nu_{1}}t_{\mu_{1}}^{\left(-\right)\nu_{1}}+g_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}t^{SP}

(4.29)

\displaystyle(t_{\mu_{12}}^{VA})_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\varepsilon_{\mu_{2}\nu_{1}}t_{\mu_{1}}^{\left(+\right)\nu_{1}}+\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}t^{PP}+2\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}\nu_{1}}t_{\mu_{2}}^{\left(-\right)\nu_{1}}-g_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}t^{SP}.

(4.30)

As mentioned at the beginning of the section, integrated amplitudes depend
exclusively on the external momentum
q
. That precludes the construction of
some 2nd-order tensors, which cancels out terms like
t^{\left(-\right)}

and
SP
. Further examination of the general form (
4.11
) allows the
identification of even amplitudes

\displaystyle t_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}^{VV}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(2t_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}^{\left(+\right)}+g_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}t^{PP})

(4.31)

\displaystyle t_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}^{AA}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(2t_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}^{\left(+\right)}-g_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}t^{SS}).

(4.32)

Hence, the integration provides the relations among odd and even amplitudes

\displaystyle(T_{\mu_{12}}^{AV})_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}}^{\hskip 8.19447pt\nu_{1}}(T_{\nu_{1}\mu_{2}}^{VV});\text{\qquad}(T_{\mu_{12}}^{AV})_{2}=-\varepsilon_{\mu_{2}}^{\hskip 8.19447pt\nu_{1}}(T_{\mu_{1}\nu_{1}}^{AA});

(4.33)

\displaystyle(T_{\mu_{12}}^{VA})_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}}^{\hskip 8.19447pt\nu_{1}}(T_{\nu_{1}\mu_{2}}^{AA});\text{\qquad}(T_{\mu_{12}}^{VA})_{2}=-\varepsilon_{\mu_{2}}^{\hskip 8.19447pt\nu_{1}}(T_{\mu_{1}\nu_{1}}^{VV}).

(4.34)

Although we did not detail, following the same steps produced both
VA

versions. These associations are directly achieved at the integrand level
using (
4.15
), the identity
\gamma_{\ast}\gamma_{\mu_{i}}=-\varepsilon_{\mu_{i}}^{\hskip 8.19447pt\nu_{1}}\gamma_{\nu_{1}}
in the
adequate position. We need a clear distinction among versions since their
comparison is not automatic for integrated amplitudes due to their diverging
character.

We also use the last identity
\gamma_{\ast}\gamma_{\left[\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\right]}=-\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}
to introduce the third
version for the discussed amplitudes. Replacing the form
\gamma_{\ast}\gamma_{\mu_{1}}\gamma_{\nu_{1}}=-\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}\nu_{1}}+g_{\mu_{1}\nu_{1}}\gamma_{\ast}
in the traces produces the results

\displaystyle(t_{\mu_{12}}^{AV})_{3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}[\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}}^{\hskip 7.0pt\nu_{1}}(t_{\nu_{1}\mu_{2}}^{VV})+\varepsilon_{\mu_{2}}^{\hskip 7.0pt\nu_{1}}(t_{\mu_{1}\nu_{1}}^{AA})]-\varepsilon_{\mu_{2}\nu_{1}}t_{\mu_{1}}^{\left(-\right)\nu_{1}}+\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}\nu_{1}}t_{\mu_{2}}^{\left(-\right)\nu_{1}},

(4.35)

\displaystyle(t_{\mu_{12}}^{VA})_{3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}[\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}}^{\hskip 7.0pt\nu_{1}}(t_{\nu_{1}\mu_{2}}^{AA})+\varepsilon_{\mu_{2}}^{\hskip 7.0pt\nu_{1}}(t_{\mu_{1}\nu_{1}}^{VV})]-\varepsilon_{\mu_{2}\nu_{1}}t_{\mu_{1}}^{\left(-\right)\nu_{1}}+\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}\nu_{1}}t_{\mu_{2}}^{\left(-\right)\nu_{1}}.

(4.36)

Since
t_{\mu}^{\left(-\right)\nu}
tensors vanish after integration,
different versions with each other as follows

(T_{\mu_{12}}^{AV})_{3}=\frac{1}{2}[(T_{\mu_{12}}^{AV})_{1}+(T_{\mu_{12}}^{AV})_{2}];\quad(T_{\mu_{12}}^{VA})_{3}=\frac{1}{2}[(T_{\mu_{12}}^{VA})_{1}+(T_{\mu_{12}}^{VA})_{2}].

(4.37)

This particular aspect receives further attention in the section (
6
). The investigation developed by the article
[
72
]

uses this version in equation (85). It illustrates how any possible
expression follows from versions one and two.

Before proceeding, we need integrated expressions. Their obtainment occurs
by replacing the results of appendix (
C
) in the integrated
versions of structures (
4.21
),(
4.22
), and (
4.24
). The scalar
two-point functions assume the forms

\displaystyle T^{PP}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle q^{2}J_{2}-2I_{\log},

(4.38)

\displaystyle T^{SS}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(4m^{2}-p^{2}\right)J_{2}+2I_{\log}.

(4.39)

And the symmetric sign tensor is

\displaystyle T_{\mu_{12}}^{\left(+\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2(\bar{J}_{2\mu_{12}}+q_{\mu_{1}}J_{2\mu_{2}})

(4.40)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\theta_{\mu_{12}}\left(q\right)\left(m^{2}J_{2}+\frac{i}{4\pi}\right)-\frac{1}{2}g_{\mu_{12}}q^{2}J_{2}+(\Delta_{2\mu_{12}}+g_{\mu_{12}}I_{\log}),

(4.41)

where
\theta_{\alpha\lambda}\left(q\right)=\left(g_{\alpha\lambda}q^{2}-q_{\alpha}q_{\lambda}\right)/q^{2}
is the transversal projector.
We put these pieces together to compound 2nd-order even tensors

\displaystyle T_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}^{VV}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\Delta_{2\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}+4\theta_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}\left(m^{2}J_{2}+\frac{i}{4\pi}\right),

(4.42)

\displaystyle T_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}^{AA}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\Delta_{2\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}+4\theta_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}\left(m^{2}J_{2}+\frac{i}{4\pi}\right)-g_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}\left(4m^{2}J_{2}\right),

(4.43)

which lead to the versions for the
AV
amplitude

\displaystyle(T_{\mu_{12}}^{AV})_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}}^{\hskip 8.19447pt\nu}\Delta_{2\mu_{2}\nu}-4\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}\nu}\theta_{\mu_{2}}^{\nu}\left(m^{2}J_{2}+\frac{i}{4\pi}\right),

(4.44)

\displaystyle(T_{\mu_{12}}^{AV})_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\varepsilon_{\mu_{2}}^{\hskip 8.19447pt\nu}\Delta_{2\mu_{1}\nu}-4\varepsilon_{\mu_{2}\nu}\theta_{\mu_{1}}^{\nu}\left(m^{2}J_{2}+\frac{i}{4\pi}\right)-\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}\left(4m^{2}J_{2}\right).

(4.45)

Two-point functions within axial RAGFs are finite and related through the
expressions

\displaystyle T_{\mu}^{PV}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-T_{\mu}^{VP}=\varepsilon_{\mu\nu}q^{\nu}\left[-2mJ_{2}\left(q\right)\right],

(4.46)

\displaystyle T_{\mu}^{PA}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-T_{\mu}^{AP}=-\varepsilon_{\mu\nu}(T^{PV})^{\nu}.

(4.47)

Whereas one-point functions are pure surface terms proportional to the
routing
k_{i}
,

T_{\mu}^{A}\left(i\right)=-\varepsilon_{\mu}^{\hskip 8.19447pt\nu_{1}}T_{\nu_{1}}^{V}\left(i\right)=2\varepsilon_{\mu}^{\hskip 8.19447pt\nu_{1}}k_{i}^{\nu_{2}}\Delta_{2\nu_{1}\nu_{2}}.

(4.48)

Even though the integrands are equivalent, the same does not apply to
integrated functions. In the case of even amplitudes (
VV
and
AA
),
expressions depend on the prescription adopted for evaluating divergences.
That also occurs for odd amplitudes (
AV
and
VA
), but they rely on the
version for the trace. Using the chiral matrix definition around the first
or the second vertexes brings implications for the index arrangement in
finite and divergent parts. This perspective produced identities originally,
but now the connection is not automatic. That becomes clear when we subtract
the
AV
expressions

\displaystyle(T_{\mu_{12}}^{AV})_{1}-(T_{\mu_{12}}^{AV})_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2(\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}\nu}\Delta_{2\mu_{2}}^{\nu}-\varepsilon_{\mu_{2}\nu}\Delta_{2\mu_{1}}^{\nu})

(4.49)

\displaystyle-4(\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}\nu_{1}}\theta_{\mu_{2}}^{\nu}-\varepsilon_{\mu_{2}\nu_{1}}\theta_{\mu_{1}}^{\nu})\left(m^{2}J_{2}+\frac{i}{4\pi}\right)+4\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}m^{2}J_{2}.

We use Schouten identities in 2D to rearrange indexes in the finite part and
in surface terms. Through the antisymmetry of the Levi-Civita tensor, we
have explicitly

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}\nu}\Delta_{2\mu_{2}}^{\nu}+\varepsilon_{\mu_{2}\mu_{1}}\Delta_{2\nu}^{\nu}+\varepsilon_{\nu\mu_{2}}\Delta_{2\mu_{1}}^{\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0=\varepsilon_{[\mu_{1}\nu}\Delta_{2\mu_{2}]}^{\nu},

(4.50)

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}\nu}\theta_{\mu_{2}}^{\nu}+\varepsilon_{\mu_{2}\mu_{1}}\theta_{\nu}^{\nu}+\varepsilon_{\nu\mu_{2}}\theta_{\mu_{1}}^{\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0=\varepsilon_{[\mu_{1}\nu}\theta_{\mu_{2}]}^{\nu}.

(4.51)

So, the difference reduces to

(T_{\mu_{12}}^{AV})_{1}-(T_{\mu_{12}}^{AV})_{2}=-\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}\left(2\Delta_{2\alpha}^{\alpha}+\frac{i}{\pi}\right).

(4.52)

The integration linearity requires this difference to vanish identically,
constraining the value of
\Delta_{2\alpha}^{\alpha}
. That represents a
link between linearity and the uniqueness of perturbative solutions. Now, we
analyze the role the surface terms play regarding the RAGFs.

4.1
Verification and Consequences of the RAGFs

We perform contractions with momentum for the integrated amplitudes to
analyze the RAGFs, starting with even functions because they relate to the
odd ones. These operations produce the difference between vector one-point
functions (
2.14
), and that occurs identically. After contracting the
integrated
VV
, finite parts cancel out due to
q^{\mu_{2}}\theta_{\mu_{2}}^{\nu}=0
, and only a surface term remains. The comparison with the
V

function (
4.48
) leads directly to the expected relation

\displaystyle q^{\mu_{1}}T_{\mu_{12}}^{VV}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2q^{\nu_{1}}\Delta_{2\mu_{2}\nu_{1}}=[T_{\mu_{2}}^{V}\left(1\right)-T_{\mu_{2}}^{V}\left(2\right)]

(4.53)

\displaystyle q^{\mu_{1}}T_{\mu_{12}}^{AA}+2mT_{\mu_{2}}^{PA}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2q^{\nu_{1}}\Delta_{2\mu_{2}\nu_{1}}=[T_{\mu_{2}}^{V}\left(1\right)-T_{\mu_{2}}^{V}\left(2\right)].

(4.54)

The same occurs with the
AA
. In this case, finite function
PA
and
surface term appear.

Now, we turn our attention to relations for odd amplitudes (
4.1
)-(
4.4
). Taking first version of
AV
(
4.44
), the contraction with
vector vertex yields

q^{\mu_{2}}(T_{\mu_{12}}^{AV})_{1}=-2\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}\nu_{1}}q^{\nu_{2}}\Delta_{2\nu_{2}}^{\nu_{1}}=[T_{\mu_{1}}^{A}\left(1\right)-T_{\mu_{1}}^{A}\left(2\right)].

(4.55)

Again, identifying the axial amplitude (
4.48
) is straightforward and does
not require conditions. That differs from the axial contraction, which needs
the rearranging of indexes,

q^{\mu_{1}}(T_{\mu_{12}}^{AV})_{1}=-2q^{\mu_{1}}\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}\nu}\Delta_{2\mu_{2}}^{\nu}-4q^{\mu_{1}}\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}\nu}\theta_{\mu_{2}}^{\nu}\left(m^{2}J_{2}+\frac{i}{4\pi}\right).

(4.56)

After employing (
4.50
)-(
4.51
), reminding that
\theta_{\nu}^{\nu}=1
, we have

q^{\mu_{1}}(T_{\mu_{12}}^{AV})_{1}=[T_{\mu_{2}}^{A}\left(k_{1}\right)-T_{\mu_{2}}^{A}\left(k_{2}\right)]-2mT_{\mu_{2}}^{PV}+\varepsilon_{\mu_{2}\nu_{1}}q^{\nu_{1}}\left(2\Delta_{2\alpha}^{\alpha}+\frac{i}{\pi}\right),

(4.57)

where

4.2
Ward Identities

In the model, we discussed the divergence of axial and vector currents (
2.20
)-(

), indicating implications through WIs for perturbative
amplitudes. The adopted strategy translates these implications as
restrictions over RAGFs, which link linearity and symmetries. This
subsection analyses such connection with particular attention to the
anomalous amplitudes, known for the impossibility of satisfying all WIs
simultaneously.

Adopting a prescription that eliminates surface terms reduces all RAGFs for
even amplitudes to the corresponding WIs. For odd amplitudes, this condition
satisfies those WIs corresponding to automatic RAGFs while violating the
others. Observe the first version of
AV
to clarify this statement.
Identifying the relations was automatic to the vector RAGF; however, the
axial RAGF gets an additional term. Hence, the zero value for the surface
term satisfies the vector WI while violating the axial WI. We see the
opposite for the second version, which breaks vector WI. Both identities are
disregarded for the third version since it is a composition of the first
two. See all the results in the Table
4.1
. The same arguments are
applied to the
VA
. Under this perspective, selecting an amplitude version
would choose the vertex for symmetry violation. Furthermore, this value for
surface terms breaks the integration linearity (in anomalous case).

Violations for vanishing surface term in each version.

Low-energy properties of finite functions are fundamental to this analysis.
Under the hypothesis that both WIs for the

amplitude apply, we

\Omega^{PV}
as being zero

(4.63)

That means the hypothesis is false. Hence, when satisfying the vector WI,
the axial WI violation is the value corresponding to the negative of
\Omega^{PV}(0)
. The other expectation (
4.10
) leads to the reciprocal:
satisfying the axial WI implies violating the vector WI.

The scenario can be understood by noting a general 2nd-order odd tensor

F_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}=\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}F_{1}+\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}\nu}q^{\nu}q_{\mu_{2}}F_{2}+\varepsilon_{\mu_{2}\nu}q^{\nu}q_{\mu_{1}}F_{3},

(4.64)

exhibits a feature when contracted with the momentum: we get two equations
that are strict consequences of its tensor properties

\displaystyle q^{\mu_{1}}F_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\mu_{2}\nu}q^{\nu}V_{1}\left(q^{2}\right)=\varepsilon_{\mu_{2}\nu}q^{\nu}\left(q^{2}F_{3}-F_{1}\right)

(4.65)

\displaystyle q^{\mu_{2}}F_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}\nu}q^{\nu}V_{2}\left(q^{2}\right)=\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}\nu}q^{\nu}\left(q^{2}F_{2}+F_{1}\right).

(4.66)

If form factors are free of kinematic singularities observed in the explicit
forms of the amplitudes, we have the implication at zero

V_{1}\left(0\right)+V_{2}\left(0\right)=0.

(4.67)

If one of the terms vanishes, the other must do so. Otherwise, if one of the

V_{i}\left(q^{2}\right)
relates to a finite function (
PV
or
VP
), an
additional constant must appear as compensation within the last equation.
Nevertheless, these statements are inconsistent with the satisfaction of
both WIs, which only occurs if linearity of integration holds with null
surface terms. Thus, the low-energy behavior of these finite functions is
the source of anomalous terms in amplitudes (
AV
-
VA
) and not their
perturbative ambiguity.

But ambiguities relate to the low-energy implications. Under the condition
of linearity and considering surface terms in the general tensor, this limit
implies the constraint
2\Delta_{2\alpha}^{\alpha}=\Omega^{PV}(0)
. Such
an aspect will be fully explored in the section considering odd triangles in
the physical dimension. Conclusions similar to those drawn here anticipate
the presence of anomalies and linearity breaking in this new circumstances.
However, now we will explore the same two-dimensional scenario but consider
a model where different species of massive fermions interact and what
generalities we can obtain from this context.
