\displaystyle q^{\mu_{1}}T_{\mu_{12}}^{VV}-2mT_{\mu_{2}}^{PA}=T_{\left(-\right)\mu_{2}}^{V}-2mT_{\mu_{2}}^{PA}

(7.198)

\displaystyle q^{\mu_{1}}T_{\mu_{12};\alpha_{1}}^{AA}

(7.199)

We have unconditional RAGF, the satisfaction established for

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}q_{\alpha_{1}}T_{\mu_{12}}^{AA}+T_{\alpha_{1}\mu_{1};\mu_{2}}^{AA}+\frac{1}{2}q_{\mu_{2}}T_{\alpha_{1}\mu_{1}}^{AA}+\frac{1}{2}g_{\alpha_{1}\mu_{1}}T_{\left(+\right)\mu_{2}}^{V}-\frac{1}{2}g_{\mu_{12}}T_{\left(+\right)\alpha_{1}}^{V}

(7.201)

\displaystyle+2m^{2}\left[g_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1}}\left(2J_{2\mu_{2}}+q_{\mu_{2}}J_{2}\right)-g_{\mu_{12}}\left(2J_{2\alpha_{1}}+q_{\alpha_{1}}J_{2}\right)\right].

The combination
2J_{2\mu_{2}}+q_{\mu_{2}}J_{2}=0
cancels out the last

7.198
) and (
7.199
) allows us to show that
the relation with indices
\alpha_{i}
are also automatically satisfied:

\displaystyle q^{\alpha_{1}}T_{\mu_{12};\alpha_{1}}^{AA}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}q^{2}T_{\mu_{12}}^{AA}+T_{\left(-\right)\mu_{2};\mu_{1}}^{V}+\frac{1}{2}q_{\mu_{1}}T_{\left(-\right)\mu_{2}}^{V}+\frac{1}{2}q_{\mu_{2}}T_{\left(+\right)\mu_{1}}^{V}

(7.202)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}g_{\mu_{12}}q^{\nu_{1}}T_{\left(+\right)\nu_{1}}^{V}+mg_{\mu_{12}}[T^{S}\left(k_{2}\right)-T^{S}\left(k_{1}\right)].

We have two additional terms corresponding to
PA
-amplitudes from RAGF with

q^{\mu_{1}}
contraction. Using (

) and (

) is easy to see
which combination

2T_{\mu_{1};\mu_{2}}^{PA}+q_{\mu_{2}}T_{\mu_{1}}^{PA}=2mq_{\mu_{2}}\left(2J_{2\mu_{1}}+q_{\mu_{1}}J_{2}\right)=0.

We have the cancelation
T^{P}\left(k_{i}\right)=0
, and the difference
between one-point functions also vanishes
T^{S}\left(k_{2}\right)-T^{S}\left(k_{1}\right)=0,
satisfying relation (
7.143
).

For the expression with four indices, we have

\displaystyle T_{\mu_{12};\alpha_{12}}^{AA}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}q_{\alpha_{1}}T_{\mu_{12};\alpha_{2}}^{AA}+T_{\alpha_{1}\mu_{2};\mu_{1}\alpha_{2}}^{AA}+\frac{1}{2}q_{\mu_{1}}T_{\alpha_{1}\mu_{2};\alpha_{2}}^{AA}

(7.203)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}g_{\alpha_{1}\mu_{2}}T_{\left(+\right)\mu_{1};\alpha_{2}}^{V}-\frac{1}{2}g_{\mu_{12}}T_{\left(+\right)\alpha_{1};\alpha_{2}}^{V}+R_{\mu_{21};\alpha_{21}}

\displaystyle+2m^{2}\left[g_{\mu_{2}\alpha_{1}}\left(2\bar{J}_{2\mu_{1}\alpha_{2}}+q_{\mu_{1}}J_{2\alpha_{2}}\right)-g_{\mu_{12}}\left(2\bar{J}_{2\alpha_{12}}+q_{\alpha_{1}}J_{2\alpha_{2}}\right)\right],

where

)

Considering the relations (

)-(
7.200
),

\displaystyle q^{\alpha_{1}}T_{\mu_{12};\alpha_{12}}^{AA}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}q^{2}T_{\mu_{12};\alpha_{2}}^{AA}+T_{\left(-\right)\mu_{2};\mu_{1}\alpha_{2}}^{V}+\frac{1}{2}q_{\mu_{1}}T_{\left(-\right)\mu_{2};\alpha_{1}}^{V}+\frac{1}{2}q_{\mu_{2}}T_{\left(+\right)\mu_{1};\alpha_{2}}^{V}-\frac{1}{2}g_{\mu_{12}}q^{\nu_{1}}T_{\left(+\right)\nu_{1};\alpha_{2}}^{V}

(7.204)

\displaystyle-m\left[(2T_{\mu_{2};\mu_{1}\alpha_{2}}^{PA}+q_{\mu_{1}}T_{\mu_{2};\alpha_{1}}^{PA})-2mq_{\mu_{2}}\left(2\bar{J}_{2\mu_{1}\alpha_{2}}+q_{\mu_{1}}J_{2\alpha_{2}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle-2m^{2}g_{\mu_{12}}\left(\Delta_{2\alpha_{2}\nu_{1}}+g_{\alpha_{2}\nu_{1}}I_{\log}\right)-2m^{2}g_{\mu_{12}}\left(2q^{\alpha_{1}}J_{2\alpha_{12}}+q^{2}J_{2\alpha_{2}}\right).

Using
2q^{\alpha_{1}}J_{2\alpha_{12}}=-q^{2}J_{2\alpha_{2}}
, last term
is null. Still, identifying other null combinations

(2T_{\mu_{2};\mu_{1}\alpha_{2}}^{PA}+q_{\mu_{1}}T_{\mu_{2};\alpha_{2}}^{PA})=2mq_{\mu_{2}}\left(2\bar{J}_{2\mu_{1}\alpha_{2}}+q_{\mu_{1}}J_{2\alpha_{2}}\right).

(7.205)

The difference between one-point scalar functions, using (
C.1
), (

), and (

),

T_{\alpha}^{S}\left(k_{2}\right)-T_{\alpha}^{S}\left(k_{1}\right)=2m[\bar{J}_{1\alpha}\left(k_{2}\right)-\bar{J}_{1\alpha}\left(k_{1}\right)-q_{\alpha}\bar{J}_{1}\left(k_{2}\right)]=-2mq^{\nu}(\Delta_{2\alpha\nu}+g_{\alpha\nu}I_{\log}).

(7.206)

The relation is satisfied directly, such that

\displaystyle q^{\alpha_{1}}T_{\mu_{12};\alpha_{12}}^{AA}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}q^{2}T_{\mu_{12};\alpha_{2}}^{AA}+T_{\left(-\right)\mu_{2};\mu_{1}\alpha_{2}}^{V}+\frac{1}{2}q_{\mu_{1}}T_{\left(-\right)\mu_{2};\alpha_{1}}^{V}+\frac{1}{2}q_{\mu_{2}}T_{\left(+\right)\mu_{1};\alpha_{2}}^{V}

(7.207)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}g_{\mu_{12}}q^{\nu_{1}}T_{\left(+\right)\nu_{1};\alpha_{2}}^{V}+mg_{\mu_{12}}[T_{\alpha_{2}}^{S}\left(k_{2}\right)-T_{\alpha_{2}}^{S}\left(k_{1}\right)].

and

The same conditions as
VV
-amplitudes will constrain relations involving
traces,

\displaystyle g^{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1}}T_{\mu_{12};\alpha_{1}}^{AA}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{\mu_{2}}^{V}\left(k_{2}\right)+mT_{\mu_{2}}^{PA}+\frac{1}{2}\left(2\Upsilon_{\mu_{2}}+q_{\mu_{2}}\Upsilon\right)

(7.208)

\displaystyle g^{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1}}T_{\mu_{12};\alpha_{12}}^{AA}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{\mu_{2};\alpha_{2}}^{V}\left(k_{2}\right)+mT_{\mu_{2};\alpha_{2}}^{PA}+\frac{1}{2}\left(2\Upsilon_{\mu_{2}\alpha_{2}}+q_{\mu_{2}}\Upsilon_{\alpha_{2}}\right).

(7.209)

Requiring that tensors calculated on (
7.107
), (
7.126
), and (
7.127
) being zero leads to its satisfaction. All relations deduced for even
amplitudes are symmetric by exchanges
\mu_{1}\leftrightarrow\mu_{2}
and

\alpha_{1}\leftrightarrow\alpha_{2}
. To make this part complete must be
noticed that if we contract with the second index
\mu_{2}
and one
derivative index, we get a superficially different expression; however,
two-point amplitudes in the RHS obey
T^{AP}=-T^{PA}
.

For instance, to obtain (
7.208
) one may use (
7.79
),

2g^{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1}}T_{\mu_{12};\alpha_{1}}^{AA}=2T_{\mu_{2}}^{V}\left(k_{2}\right)+2m(T_{\mu_{2}}^{SV}-4mJ_{2\mu_{2}})+\left(2\Upsilon_{\mu_{2}}+q_{\mu_{2}}\Upsilon\right).

(7.210)

Furthermore, notice that the identity
(T_{\mu_{2}}^{SV}-4mJ_{2\mu_{2}})=2mq_{\mu_{2}}J_{2}=T_{\mu_{2}}^{PA}
returns the first equation we
showed. The deduction steps for two derivative indices are unchanged. One
could also invoke Eq. (
7.201
) for trading between one derivative and
one matrix index; thus, taking the trace, there will appear a RAGF to inner
contractions (with matrix indices), which in turn are identically satisfied
as demonstrated previously. Therefore, we employ that derivation in the
equation below

2g^{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1}}T_{\mu_{12};\alpha_{1}}^{AA}=g^{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1}}(2T_{\alpha_{1}\mu_{1};\mu_{2}}^{AA}+q_{\mu_{2}}T_{\alpha_{1}\mu_{1}}^{AA})-q^{\mu_{1}}T_{\mu_{12}}^{AA}+T_{\left(+\right)\mu_{2}}^{V}+4m^{2}(2J_{2\mu_{2}}+q_{\mu_{2}}J_{2}).

(7.211)

Reminding that
T_{\mu_{2}}^{SV}=2m\left(2J_{2\mu_{2}}+q_{\mu_{2}}J_{2}\right)
, final expression assumes the form

2g^{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1}}T_{\mu_{12};\alpha_{1}}^{AA}=g^{\nu_{12}}(2T_{\nu_{12};\mu_{2}}^{AA}+q_{\mu_{2}}T_{\nu_{12}}^{AA})+2T_{\mu_{2}}^{V}\left(k_{2}\right)+2mT_{\mu_{2}}^{PA}+2mT_{\mu_{2}}^{SV}.

(7.212)

After that, we transform
AA
into
VV
on the LHS following (
7.208
).

We finished calculating all the amplitudes and RAGF of even amplitudes. The
relations involving momentum with matrix indices and derivatives are all
automatically satisfied. However, in the case of traces, we saw that two
groups of amplitudes presented violations by the same terms.

7.4
Odd Amplitudes: RAGFs

For odd amplitudes
AV
-
VA
, internal contractions are different by the
vertex character; specifying the contraction with the axial vertex is
necessary. As we saw, these relations are not satisfied without restriction,
and the presence of an anomalous term is due to the existence of a chiral
anomaly at this vertex,

\displaystyle q^{\mu_{1}}t_{\mu_{12};\alpha_{1}}^{AV}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[t_{\mu_{2};\alpha_{1}}^{A}\left(k_{1}\right)-t_{\mu_{2};\alpha_{1}}^{A}\left(k_{2}\right)]-2mt_{\mu_{2};\alpha_{1}}^{PV}=t_{\left(-\right)\mu_{2};\alpha_{1}}^{A}-2mt_{\mu_{2};\alpha_{1}}^{PV}

(7.213)

\displaystyle q^{\mu_{1}}t_{\mu_{12};\alpha_{12}}^{AV}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[t_{\mu_{2};\alpha_{12}}^{A}\left(k_{1}\right)-t_{\mu_{2};\alpha_{12}}^{A}\left(k_{2}\right)]-2mt_{\mu_{2};\alpha_{12}}^{PV}=t_{\left(-\right)\mu_{2};\alpha_{12}}^{A}-2mt_{\mu_{2};\alpha_{12}}^{PV},

(7.214)

where
t_{\left(-\right)\mu_{2};\alpha_{1}}^{A}
and
t_{\left(-\right)\mu_{2};\alpha_{12}}^{A}
are associated with difference of axial
one-point function,

\displaystyle t_{\left(-\right)\mu_{2};\alpha_{1}}^{A}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle t_{\mu_{2};\alpha_{1}}^{A}\left(k_{1}\right)-t_{\mu_{2};\alpha_{1}}^{A}\left(k_{2}\right)

(7.215)

\displaystyle t_{\left(-\right)\mu_{2};\alpha_{12}}^{A}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle t_{\mu_{2};\alpha_{12}}^{A}\left(k_{1}\right)-t_{\mu_{2};\alpha_{12}}^{A}\left(k_{2}\right).

(7.216)

Relations for vectorial vertexes are given by

\displaystyle q^{\mu_{2}}t_{\mu_{12};\alpha_{1}}^{AV}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle t_{\mu_{1};\alpha_{1}}^{A}\left(k_{1}\right)-t_{\mu_{1};\alpha_{1}}^{A}\left(k_{2}\right)=t_{\left(-\right)\mu_{1};\alpha_{1}}^{A}

(7.217)

\displaystyle q^{\mu_{2}}t_{\mu_{12};\alpha_{12}}^{AV}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle t_{\mu_{1};\alpha_{12}}^{A}\left(k_{1}\right)-t_{\mu_{1};\alpha_{12}}^{A}\left(k_{2}\right)=t_{\left(-\right)\mu_{1};\alpha_{12}}^{A}.

(7.218)

Two identities can be constructed in external contractions, as explored in
the even ones. If we insert the factor (
7.131
) next to the first vertex
we will obtain

\displaystyle q^{\alpha_{1}}t_{\mu_{12};\alpha_{1}}^{AV}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}q^{2}t_{\mu_{12}}^{AV}+t_{\left(-\right)\mu_{2};\mu_{1}}^{A}+\frac{1}{2}q_{\mu_{1}}t_{\left(-\right)\mu_{2}}^{A}+\frac{1}{2}q_{\mu_{2}}t_{\left(+\right)\mu_{1}}^{A}-\frac{1}{2}g_{\mu_{12}}q^{\alpha_{1}}t_{\left(+\right)\alpha_{1}}^{A}

(7.219)

\displaystyle+m\varepsilon_{\mu_{12}}[t^{S}\left(k_{2}\right)-t^{S}\left(k_{1}\right)]+mg_{\mu_{12}}[t^{P}\left(k_{2}\right)+t^{P}\left(k_{1}\right)].

The notation
t_{\left(+\right)\mu_{1}}^{A}
is associated with the sum
of the axial one-point function, namely

t_{\left(+\right)\mu_{1}}^{A}=t_{\mu_{1}}^{A}\left(k_{1}\right)+t_{\mu_{1}}^{A}\left(k_{2}\right).

(7.220)

But if we use the same identity around the second vertex, the relations are

q^{\alpha_{1}}t_{\mu_{12};\alpha_{1}}^{AV}=-\frac{1}{2}q^{2}t_{\mu_{12}}^{AV}+t_{\left(-\right)\mu_{1};\mu_{2}}^{A}+\frac{1}{2}q_{\mu_{2}}t_{\left(-\right)\mu_{1}}^{A}+\frac{1}{2}q_{\mu_{1}}t_{\left(+\right)\mu_{2}}^{A}-\frac{1}{2}g_{\mu_{12}}q^{\rho}t_{\left(+\right)\rho}^{A}.

(7.221)

The same to the four-indexes amplitudes, adding one index more. In addition
to the relations (
7.219
) having additional terms when compared to (
7.221
). The roles of indices
\mu_{1}
and
\mu_{2}
are different.
We will see its consequences in the course of this investigation.

In contractions with the metric, the indices
\mu_{1}
and
\mu_{2}
give
us different relations:

\displaystyle g^{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1}}t_{\mu_{12};\alpha_{1}}^{AV}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle t_{\mu_{2}}^{A}\left(k_{2}\right)+mt_{\mu_{2}}^{PV}

(7.222)

\displaystyle g^{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1}}t_{\mu_{12};\alpha_{12}}^{AV}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle t_{\mu_{2};\alpha_{2}}^{A}\left(k_{2}\right)+mt_{\mu_{2};\alpha_{2}}^{PV}.

(7.223)

\displaystyle g^{\mu_{2}\alpha_{1}}t_{\mu_{12};\alpha_{1}}^{AV}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle t_{\mu_{1}}^{A}\left(k_{2}\right)+mt_{\mu_{1}}^{AS}

(7.224)

\displaystyle g^{\mu_{2}\alpha_{2}}t_{\mu_{12};\alpha_{12}}^{AV}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle t_{\mu_{1};\alpha_{1}}^{A}\left(k_{2}\right)+mt_{\mu_{1};\alpha_{1}}^{AS}.

(7.225)

The relations for
VA
amplitudes are analogous and complementary.

These relations, it is possible to establish all relations that come from
the contractions for the complete expression of Gravitational Amplitude, see
(
7.48
). Their violations or satisfactions are closely related to
the symmetries to be determined. From the view of our strategy, these
relations establish a minimum consistency test of amplitudes after
integration. In other words, if they are satisfied, the linearity of the
integration operation is maintained. Since we expect that when we explicitly
calculate an amplitude, whatever calculation procedure is used, the
contraction of the final result with the external momentum for each
amplitude vertex should reproduce the expected RAGF. Otherwise, we can
establish some relations of amplitude violations.

As we have seen in sections for even amplitudes, relations with momenta
contractions are unconditionally satisfied. It was not necessary to impose
any condition regarding divergent content. However, the case is somewhat
different for odd amplitudes. This relation type is not trivially satisfied.
Furthermore, we will show that presence of terms (
7.107
), (
7.126
),
and (
7.127
) violate different contractions depending on
AV
-versions.

7.4.1
Internal Contractions:
q^{\mu}T_{\mu\nu;\sigma}^{AV}
and
q^{\mu}T_{\mu\nu;\sigma\lambda}^{AV}
and
V\leftrightarrow A

Derived in Chapter (
4
), we have that contraction with the axial
vertex for the first version of
AV
-amplitudes in (
4.57
) is violated.
The mechanism develops similarly for
q^{\mu_{2}}
contraction; the index
meets the index inside
VV
-amplitude and, through its identities, implies
automatic preservation of RAGF,

\displaystyle q^{\mu_{1}}(T_{\mu_{12}}^{AV})_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{\left(-\right)\mu_{2}}^{A}-2mT_{\mu_{2}}^{PV}+\varepsilon_{\mu_{2}\mu_{1}}q^{\mu_{1}}\Upsilon

(7.226)

\displaystyle q^{\mu_{2}}(T_{\mu_{12}}^{AV})_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{\left(-\right)\mu_{1}}^{A}.

(7.227)

The second version works oppositely and satisfies relations established for
q^{\mu_{1}}.
Just because the
AA
automatically satisfies its RAGF, the
relation for index
\mu_{2}
follows with an additional term, as expected.
To see this, we use the link connecting versions and obtain

\displaystyle q^{\mu_{1}}(T_{\mu_{12}}^{AV})_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{\left(-\right)\mu_{2}}^{A}-2mT_{\mu_{2}}^{PV}

(7.228)

\displaystyle q^{\mu_{2}}(T_{\mu_{12}}^{AV})_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{\left(-\right)\mu_{1}}^{A}+\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}\nu}q^{\nu}\Upsilon.

(7.229)

Hence, to this relation type and for amplitudes with derivative indices
also, the RAGF coming from
q^{\mu_{i}}
contraction is directly verified
if a version is
j=i
and needs manipulation in its indices given by
relations among versions (
7.108
) if
i=j
. In the second case arises
factors
\{\Upsilon,\Upsilon_{\alpha},\Upsilon_{\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}}\}
that we developed as specific tensors connecting two basic
versions.

Elements that we have elaborated on are enough to establish relations for
both contractions
q^{\mu_{i}}
and both versions
\left\{\left(AV\right)_{i},\left(VA\right)_{i}\right\}
and any number of derivative indices.
To do this, first, we call attention to specific results
T_{\mu}^{PA}=-T_{\mu}^{AP}
and
T_{\mu}^{PV}=-T_{\mu}^{VP}
. This result is
valid irrespective of their finite character since they do not depend on the
traces employed in their calculation. Therefore, they are also helpful for
structures with more indices. The required results are listed below

\displaystyle-\varepsilon_{\mu_{2}}^{\text{\hskip 7.97224pt}\nu_{1}}T_{\nu_{1}}^{PA}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{\mu_{2}}^{PV}=-\varepsilon_{\mu_{2}}^{\text{\hskip 7.97224pt}\nu_{1}}(2mq_{\nu_{1}}J_{2})

(7.230)

\displaystyle-\varepsilon_{\mu_{2}}^{\text{\hskip 7.97224pt}\nu_{1}}T_{\nu_{1};\alpha_{1}}^{PA}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{\mu_{2};\alpha_{1}}^{PV}=-\varepsilon_{\mu_{2}}^{\text{\hskip 7.97224pt}\nu_{1}}(2mq_{\nu_{1}}J_{2\alpha_{1}})

(7.231)

\displaystyle-\varepsilon_{\mu_{2}}^{\text{\hskip 7.97224pt}\nu_{1}}T_{\nu_{1};\alpha_{12}}^{PA}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{\mu_{2};\alpha_{12}}^{PV}=-\varepsilon_{\mu_{2}}^{\text{\hskip 7.97224pt}\nu_{1}}(2mq_{\nu_{1}}\bar{J}_{2\alpha_{12}}).

(7.232)

General structures of RAGFs are obtained by explicitly calculating all
amplitudes

\displaystyle q^{\mu_{i}}(T_{\mu_{12}}^{AV})_{j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{\left(-\right)\mu_{k}}^{A}-\delta_{1,i}(2mT_{\mu_{2}}^{PV})+\delta_{i,j}\left(\varepsilon_{\mu_{k}\nu}q^{\nu}\Upsilon\right)

(7.233)

\displaystyle q^{\mu_{i}}(T_{\mu_{12};\alpha_{1}}^{AV})_{j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{\left(-\right)\mu_{k};\alpha_{1}}^{A}-\delta_{1,i}(2mT_{\mu_{2};\alpha_{1}}^{PV})+\delta_{i,j}\left(\varepsilon_{\mu_{k}\nu}q^{\nu}\Upsilon_{\alpha_{1}}\right)

(7.234)

\displaystyle q^{\mu_{i}}(T_{\mu_{12};\alpha_{12}}^{AV})_{j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{\left(-\right)\mu_{k};\alpha_{12}}^{A}-\delta_{1,i}(2mT_{\mu_{2};\alpha_{12}}^{PV})+\delta_{i,j}\left(\varepsilon_{\mu_{k}\nu}q^{\nu}\Upsilon_{\alpha_{12}}\right),

(7.235)

i,j,k=\left\{1,2\right\}
with
k\not=i
, and
\delta_{ij}
is Kronecker
delta equal to one if
i=j
and zero otherwise. The formulae encode when one
contracts with
q^{\mu_{i}}
the version
j=i
, i.e., with vertex index
where the version was defined, there is a
\Upsilon
-factor, not if there
is no match
i\not=j
,
\delta_{ij}
encodes these behaviors; it also
captures if contraction has a
PV
function (see
\delta_{1,i}
). Note that
when
i\not=j,
there is no constraint over surface terms; in complementary
cases, constraints are to be studied. They happen over the same
\Upsilon
-factors as even amplitude traces; however, not in combination as in
Subsection (
7.3.3
).

To complete, we ought to remind condition-less relations among
VA
and
AV
-tensors:

\displaystyle T_{\mu_{12}}^{VA}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{\mu_{12}}^{AV}+4m^{2}\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}J_{2}

(7.236)

\displaystyle T_{\mu_{12};\alpha_{1}}^{VA}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{\mu_{12};\alpha_{1}}^{AV}+4m^{2}\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}J_{2\alpha_{1}}

\displaystyle T_{\mu_{12};\alpha_{12}}^{VA}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{\mu_{12};\alpha_{12}}^{AV}+4m^{2}\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}\bar{J}_{2\alpha_{12}}.

As they are valid for any version, we did not use indices. Despite this, we
could also study the unicity relations
(T_{\mu_{12}}^{VA})_{2}-(T_{\mu_{12}}^{VA})_{1}=-\varepsilon_{\mu_{21}}\Upsilon,
and so on for higher
rank. In parallel to previous deductions, we can cast the pattern of
contractions related to the RAGFs explicitly and in a systematic form as
AV

versions:

\displaystyle q^{\mu_{i}}(T_{\mu_{12}}^{VA})_{j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{\left(-\right)\mu_{k}}^{A}+\delta_{2,i}(2mT_{\mu_{2}}^{VP})+\delta_{i,j}\left(\varepsilon_{\mu_{k}\nu}q^{\nu}\Upsilon\right)

(7.237)

\displaystyle q^{\mu_{i}}(T_{\mu_{12};\alpha_{1}}^{VA})_{j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{\left(-\right)\mu_{k};\alpha_{1}}^{A}+\delta_{2,i}(2mT_{\mu_{2};\alpha_{1}}^{VP})+\delta_{i,j}\left(\varepsilon_{\mu_{k}\nu}q^{\nu}\Upsilon_{\alpha_{1}}\right)

(7.238)

\displaystyle q^{\mu_{i}}(T_{\mu_{12};\alpha_{12}}^{VA})_{j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{\left(-\right)\mu_{k};\alpha_{12}}^{A}+\delta_{2,i}(2mT_{\mu_{2};\alpha_{12}}^{VP})+\delta_{i,j}\left(\varepsilon_{\mu_{k}\nu}q^{\nu}\Upsilon_{\alpha_{12}}\right).

(7.239)

It is worth noticing that
\delta_{2,i}
makes precise VP functions appear
in
q^{\mu_{2}}
-relations. Once more, this is a summary of the results; an
important point is the appearance of conditioning factors in relations
corresponding to the vertices around those we used the chiral matrix
definition. As demonstrated in sections, that is equivalent to substituting (
4.15
).

7.4.2
External Contractions:
q^{\sigma}T_{\mu\nu;\sigma}^{AV}
and
q^{\sigma}T_{\mu\nu;\sigma\lambda}^{AV}
and
V\leftrightarrow A

Treating relations involving derivative indices as we did for the even case
is possible. The
VV
amplitudes can be manipulated and written through the
identities (
7.166
) and (
7.171
); when we exchange any
derivative index for a matrix index,

t_{\mu_{12};\alpha_{1}}^{VV}=-\frac{1}{2}q_{\alpha_{1}}t_{\mu_{12}}^{VV}+t_{\alpha_{1}\mu_{2};\mu_{1}}^{VV}+\frac{1}{2}q_{\mu_{1}}t_{\alpha_{1}\mu_{2}}^{VV}+\frac{1}{2}g_{\alpha_{1}\mu_{2}}t_{\left(+\right)\mu_{1}}^{V}-\frac{1}{2}g_{\mu_{12}}t_{\left(+\right)\alpha_{1}}^{V}+r_{\mu_{12};\alpha_{1}}.

The exchange effect is equally valid for
\alpha_{1}\leftrightarrow\mu_{2}
, resulting
\mu_{1}\leftrightarrow\mu_{2}
in the equation above.

We can get relations for odd amplitudes obtained of
VV
-amplitudes.
Appropriately exchanging indices and multiplying by tensor
-\varepsilon_{\mu_{i}}^{\text{ \ }\nu_{1}}
leads us to unconditional identities

\displaystyle(T_{\mu_{12};\alpha_{1}}^{AV})_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}q_{\alpha_{1}}(T_{\mu_{12}}^{AV})_{1}+(T_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1};\mu_{2}}^{AV})_{1}+\frac{1}{2}q_{\mu_{2}}(T_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1}}^{AV})_{1}

(7.240)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}T_{\left(+\right)\alpha_{1}}^{V}-\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1}}T_{\left(+\right)\mu_{2}}^{V}

\displaystyle(T_{\mu_{12};\alpha_{1}}^{VA})_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}q_{\alpha_{1}}(T_{\mu_{12}}^{VA})_{2}+(T_{\alpha_{1}\mu_{2};\mu_{1}}^{VA})_{2}+\frac{1}{2}q_{\mu_{1}}(T_{\alpha_{1}\mu_{2}}^{VA})_{2}

(7.241)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}T_{\left(+\right)\alpha_{1}}^{V}-\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon_{\mu_{2}\alpha_{1}}T_{\left(+\right)\mu_{1}}^{V}.

It is necessary to remember the versions of amplitudes in terms of
AA
(
7.201
) and (
7.203
). Follow the other identities satisfied by odd
amplitudes,

\displaystyle(T_{\mu_{12};\alpha_{1}}^{AV})_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}q_{\alpha_{1}}(T_{\mu_{12}}^{AV})_{2}+(T_{\alpha_{1}\mu_{2};\mu_{1}}^{AV})_{2}+\frac{1}{2}q_{\mu_{1}}(T_{\alpha_{1}\mu_{2}}^{AV})_{2}

(7.242)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}T_{\left(+\right)\alpha_{1}}^{V}-\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon_{\mu_{2}\alpha_{1}}T_{\left(+\right)\mu_{1}}^{V}

\displaystyle(T_{\mu_{12};\alpha_{1}}^{VA})_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}q_{\alpha_{1}}(T_{\mu_{12}}^{VA})_{1}+(T_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1};\mu_{2}}^{VA})_{1}+\frac{1}{2}q_{\mu_{2}}(T_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1}}^{VA})_{1}

(7.243)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}T_{\left(+\right)\alpha_{1}}^{V}-\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1}}T_{\left(+\right)\mu_{2}}^{V}.

By construction, we will see that these identities will always be satisfied.
Starting to analyze this trajectory by the first version. From expression (
7.240
), we have

\displaystyle q^{\alpha_{1}}(T_{\mu_{12};\alpha_{1}}^{AV})_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}q^{2}(T_{\mu_{12}}^{AV})_{1}+q^{\alpha_{1}}(T_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1};\mu_{2}}^{AV})_{1}+\frac{1}{2}q_{\mu_{2}}[q^{\alpha_{1}}(T_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1}}^{AV})_{1}]

(7.244)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}q^{\alpha_{1}}T_{\left(+\right)\alpha_{1}}^{V}-\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1}}q^{\alpha_{1}}T_{\left(+\right)\mu_{2}}^{V}.

Identifying relations with internal indices that are satisfied for version
one yields

\displaystyle q^{\alpha_{1}}(T_{\mu_{12};\alpha_{1}}^{AV})_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}q^{2}(T_{\mu_{12}}^{AV})_{1}+T_{\left(-\right)\mu_{1};\mu_{2}}^{A}+\frac{1}{2}q_{\mu_{2}}T_{\left(-\right)\mu_{1}}^{A}

(7.245)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}q^{\alpha_{1}}T_{\left(+\right)\alpha_{1}}^{V}-\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1}}q^{\alpha_{1}}T_{\left(+\right)\mu_{2}}^{V}.

As in the last line, there is no direct identification of one-point
vectorial functions with axial ones. We need to use the Schouten identity
just like

[\varepsilon_{\mu_{12}}q^{\nu_{2}}-q^{\nu_{1}}\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}\nu_{1}}\delta_{\mu_{2}}^{\nu_{2}}]T_{\left(+\right)\nu_{2}}^{V}=q_{\mu_{1}}T_{\left(+\right)\mu_{2}}^{A}-g_{\mu_{12}}q^{\nu_{1}}T_{\left(+\right)\nu_{1}}^{A}.

(7.246)

Thus, replacing in equation above, we obtain

\displaystyle q^{\alpha_{1}}(T_{\mu_{12};\alpha_{1}}^{AV})_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}q^{2}(T_{\mu_{12}}^{AV})_{1}+T_{\left(-\right)\mu_{1};\mu_{2}}^{A}+\frac{1}{2}q_{\mu_{2}}T_{\left(-\right)\mu_{1}}^{A}

(7.247)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}g_{\mu_{12}}q^{\nu_{1}}T_{\left(+\right)\nu_{1}}^{A}+\frac{1}{2}q_{\mu_{1}}T_{\left(+\right)\mu_{2}}^{A}.

That is the relation obtained around the second vertex (
7.221
). The
reason for satisfaction is that index replaced by
\alpha_{i}
always
appears as the one amplitude version, and
q^{\alpha_{i}}
is always
complimentary. In the case of
q^{\alpha_{1}}(T_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1};\mu_{2}}^{AV})_{1}
and
q^{\alpha_{1}}(T_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1}}^{AV})_{1}
,
the RAGF for vectorial indices are automatically satisfied. The same happens
contraction for
(T_{\mu_{12};\alpha_{1}}^{VA})_{1}
: contractions with
axial indices are satisfied, and the additional finite part cancels out

-m(2T_{\mu_{2};\mu_{1}}^{PV}+q_{\mu_{1}}T_{\mu_{2}}^{PV})=2m^{2}\varepsilon_{\mu_{2}\nu_{1}}q^{\nu_{1}}(2J_{2\mu_{1}}+q_{\mu_{1}}J_{2})=0.

(7.248)

So, we have the RAGF satisfied around the second vertex.

Violations occur precisely in relations established around the vertex
associated with version: first vertex, thus first version, second vertex,
second version. For example, the same manipulations lead to

\displaystyle q^{\alpha_{1}}(T_{\mu_{12};\alpha_{1}}^{AV})_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}q^{2}(T_{\mu_{12}}^{AV})_{2}+T_{\left(-\right)\mu_{2};\mu_{1}}^{A}+\frac{1}{2}q_{\mu_{1}}T_{\left(-\right)\mu_{2}}^{A}

(7.249)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}[\varepsilon_{\mu_{21}}q^{\nu_{2}}-\varepsilon_{\mu_{2}\nu_{1}}\delta_{\mu_{1}}^{\nu_{2}}q^{\nu_{1}}]T_{\left(+\right)\nu_{2}}^{V}+m(2T_{\mu_{2};\mu_{1}}^{PV}+q_{\mu_{1}}T_{\mu_{2}}^{PV}).

Applying Schouten identity in the last line and canceling out additional
finite parts,

\displaystyle q^{\alpha_{1}}(T_{\mu_{12};\alpha_{1}}^{AV})_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}q^{2}(T_{\mu_{12}}^{AV})_{2}+T_{\left(-\right)\mu_{2};\mu_{1}}^{A}+\frac{1}{2}q_{\mu_{1}}T_{\left(-\right)\mu_{2}}^{A}

(7.250)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}q_{\mu_{2}}T_{\left(+\right)\mu_{1}}^{A}-\frac{1}{2}g_{\mu_{12}}q^{\nu_{1}}T_{\left(+\right)\nu_{1}}^{A}.

It satisfies the relation deduced around the first vertex (
7.219
) but
does not satisfy the relation deduced around the second (
7.221
).
Remembering that massive terms do not contribute because they are null for
these amplitudes.

Taking advantage of equations (
7.108
) and (
7.109
) incorporate
uniqueness conditions and invariably connect them, we will have the possible
violating term:

\left.q^{\alpha_{1}}(T_{\mu_{12};\alpha_{1}}^{AV})_{1}\right|^{\mathrm{viol}}=-\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon_{\mu_{12}}q^{\alpha_{1}}\left(2\Upsilon_{\alpha_{1}}+q_{\alpha_{1}}\Upsilon\right).

However, let us consider that the expression obtained around the second
vertex is valid (
7.221
). The same type of violation will be present
in the second version, and the first will be automatically satisfied.

For amplitude with two derivative factors, the calculation follows equation (
7.203
),

\displaystyle(T_{\mu_{12};\alpha_{12}}^{AV})_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}q_{\alpha_{1}}(T_{\mu_{12};\alpha_{2}}^{AV})_{2}+(T_{\alpha_{1}\mu_{2};\mu_{1}\alpha_{2}}^{AV})_{2}+\frac{1}{2}q_{\mu_{1}}(T_{\alpha_{1}\mu_{2};\alpha_{2}}^{AV})_{2}

(7.251)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon_{\mu_{2}\alpha_{1}}T_{\left(+\right)\mu_{1};\alpha_{2}}^{V}-\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}T_{\left(+\right)\alpha_{1};\alpha_{2}}^{V}-\varepsilon_{\mu_{2}}^{\text{ \ \ }\nu_{1}}R_{\mu_{1}\nu_{1};\alpha_{12}}

\displaystyle-2m^{2}\varepsilon_{\mu_{2}\alpha_{1}}(2\bar{J}_{2\mu_{1}\alpha_{2}}+q_{\mu_{1}}J_{2\alpha_{2}})-2m^{2}\varepsilon_{\mu_{12}}(2\bar{J}_{2\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}}+q_{\alpha_{1}}J_{2\alpha_{2}}),

where
R_{\mu_{1}\nu_{1};\alpha_{12}}
is defined in (
7.173
) and null
by contraction. It is simple to show that version one, using (
7.110
),
the possible violating term is given by

\left.q^{\alpha_{1}}(T_{\mu_{12};\alpha_{12}}^{AV})_{1}\right|^{\mathrm{viol}}=-\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon_{\mu_{12}}q^{\nu}\left(2\Upsilon_{\alpha_{2}\nu}+q_{\nu}\Upsilon_{\alpha_{2}}\right).

(7.252)

The same analysis leads to similar conclusions for the second version of
amplitudes if the relation around the second vertex is the reference.

7.4.3
Metric Contractions:
g^{\mu\sigma}T_{\mu\nu;\sigma}^{AV}
and
g^{\mu\sigma}T_{\mu\nu;\sigma\lambda}^{AV}

Finally, the last relation we need to calculate. Once again, we will make
use of relations through a reorganization of terms that can be seen from

\displaystyle(T_{\mu_{12};\alpha_{1}}^{AV})_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}q_{\alpha_{1}}(T_{\mu_{12}}^{AV})_{1}+(T_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1};\mu_{2}}^{AV})_{1}+\frac{1}{2}q_{\mu_{2}}(T_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1}}^{AV})_{1}

(7.253)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}T_{\left(+\right)\alpha_{1}}^{V}-\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1}}T_{\left(+\right)\mu_{2}}^{V}.

Starting by contracting the expression above with
g^{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1}}
,

g^{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1}}(T_{\mu_{12};\alpha_{1}}^{AV})_{1}=\frac{1}{2}[-q^{\mu_{1}}(T_{\mu_{12}}^{AV})_{1}+T_{\left(+\right)\mu_{2}}^{A}]+\frac{1}{2}g^{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1}}[2(T_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1};\mu_{2}}^{AV})_{1}+q_{\mu_{2}}(T_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1}}^{AV})_{1}].

(7.254)

At this point, it is straightforward to note that the
AV
amplitude can be
written as

g^{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1}}[2(T_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1};\mu_{2}}^{AV})_{1}+q_{\mu_{2}}(T_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1}}^{AV})_{1}]=-\varepsilon^{\alpha_{1}\nu_{1}}[2T_{\nu_{1}\alpha_{1};\mu_{2}}^{VV}+q_{\mu_{2}}T_{\nu_{1}\alpha_{1}}^{VV}]=0.

(7.255)

It is canceled because complete
VV
-amplitudes are symmetric in its first
indices, finite and non-finite parts. Using (
7.226
) for
q^{\mu_{1}}(T_{\mu_{12}}^{AV})_{1}
, where appear
\Upsilon=(2\Delta_{2\rho}^{\rho}+i/\pi)
, follows

g^{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1}}(T_{\mu_{12};\alpha_{1}}^{AV})_{1}=mT_{\mu_{2}}^{PV}+T_{\mu_{2}}^{A}\left(k_{2}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon_{\mu_{2}\nu}q^{\nu}\Upsilon.

(7.256)

For
g^{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1}}(T_{\mu_{12};\alpha_{1}}^{AV})_{2}
we also
find this relation conditioned. Using the equation that connects two
versions (
7.108
) and (
7.109
), we obtain the desired relation

g^{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1}}(T_{\mu_{12};\alpha_{1}}^{AV})_{2}=mT_{\mu_{2}}^{PV}+T_{\mu_{2}}^{A}\left(k_{2}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon_{\mu_{2}\nu}\left(2\Upsilon^{\nu}+q^{\nu}\Upsilon\right).

(7.257)

An alternative way to extract this information, valid whenever the index of
inner vertices is not the one used to define the version, is to invoke the
equation derived from
VV
or
AA
functions and multiply them by an
adequate tensor. Explicitly, we multiplied the equation below by
-\varepsilon_{\mu_{2}}^{\hskip 8.19447pt\nu},

2g^{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1}}T_{\mu_{1}\nu;\alpha_{1}}^{AA}-2T_{\nu}^{V}\left(k_{2}\right)=2mT_{\nu}^{PA}+\left(2\Upsilon_{\nu}+q_{\nu}\Upsilon\right).

(7.258)

By definition, it follows

2g^{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1}}(T_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2};\alpha_{1}}^{AV})_{2}=2mT_{\mu_{2}}^{PV}+2T_{\mu_{2}}^{A}\left(k_{2}\right)-\varepsilon_{\mu_{2}\nu}\left(2\Upsilon^{\nu}+q^{\nu}\Upsilon\right).

(7.259)

Contracting with
g^{\mu_{2}\alpha_{1}}
, the application of equation (
7.242
) leads to

2g^{\mu_{2}\alpha_{1}}(T_{\mu_{12};\alpha_{1}}^{AV})_{2}=-q^{\mu_{2}}(T_{\mu_{12}}^{AV})_{2}+T_{\left(+\right)\mu_{1}}^{A}+g^{\nu_{2}\nu_{1}}[2(T_{\nu_{12};\mu_{1}}^{AV})_{2}+q_{\mu_{1}}(T_{\nu_{12}}^{AV})_{2}].

The last line drops out by index symmetry in the
AA
-amplitudes. Then using
(
7.234
) and finite piece
T_{\mu_{1}}^{AS}=0
, follows

g^{\mu_{2}\alpha_{1}}(T_{\mu_{12};\alpha_{1}}^{AV})_{2}=mT_{\mu_{1}}^{AS}+T_{\mu_{1}}^{A}\left(k_{2}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}\nu}q^{\nu}\Upsilon

(7.260)

For version one, we also find this relation violated

g^{\mu_{2}\alpha_{1}}(T_{\mu_{12};\alpha_{1}}^{AV})_{1}=mT_{\mu_{1}}^{AS}+T_{\mu_{1}}^{A}\left(k_{2}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}\nu}\left(2\Upsilon^{\nu}+q^{\nu}\Upsilon\right).

(7.261)

The 4th-rank amplitudes with two external indices are easily obtained
following the same steps. Thus, we have the list of equations below,

\displaystyle g^{\mu_{i}\alpha_{1}}(T_{\mu_{12};\alpha_{1}}^{AV})_{j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{\mu_{k}}^{A}\left(k_{2}\right)+m(\delta_{i,1}T_{\mu_{k}}^{PV}+\delta_{i,2}T_{\mu_{k}}^{AS})

(7.262)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon_{\mu_{k}\nu}\left[q^{\nu}\Upsilon+2\left(1-\delta_{i,j}\right)\Upsilon^{\nu}\right]

\displaystyle g^{\mu_{i}\alpha_{1}}(T_{\mu_{12};\alpha_{12}}^{AV})_{j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{\mu_{k};\alpha_{2}}^{A}\left(k_{2}\right)+m(\delta_{i,1}T_{\mu_{k};\alpha_{2}}^{PV}+\delta_{i,2}T_{\mu_{k};\alpha_{2}}^{AS})

(7.263)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon_{\mu_{k}\nu}\left[q^{\nu}\Upsilon_{\alpha_{2}}+2\left(1-\delta_{i,j}\right)\Upsilon_{\alpha_{2}}^{\nu}\right],

where
\left\{i,j,k\right\}=\left\{1,2\right\}
,
k\not=i.
The
Kronecker delta guarantees that only correct terms appear in each equation;
note that they reproduce all the previous equations. Additionally, for the
VA
amplitude, we have

\displaystyle g^{\mu_{i}\alpha_{1}}(T_{\mu_{12};\alpha_{1}}^{VA})_{j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{\mu_{k}}^{A}\left(k_{2}\right)+m(\delta_{i,1}T_{\mu_{2}}^{AS}-\delta_{i,2}T_{\mu_{1}}^{VP})

(7.264)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon_{\mu_{k}\nu}\left[q^{\nu}\Upsilon+2\left(1-\delta_{i,j}\right)\Upsilon^{\nu}\right]

\displaystyle g^{\mu_{i}\alpha_{1}}(T_{\mu_{12};\alpha_{12}}^{VA})_{j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle T_{\mu_{k};\alpha_{2}}^{A}\left(k_{2}\right)+m(\delta_{i,1}T_{\mu_{2};\alpha_{2}}^{AS}-\delta_{i,2}T_{\mu_{1};\alpha_{1}}^{VP})

(7.265)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon_{\mu_{k}\nu}\left[q^{\nu}\Upsilon_{\alpha_{2}}+2\left(1-\delta_{i,j}\right)\Upsilon_{\alpha_{2}}^{\nu}\right],

where
T_{\mu_{1};\alpha_{1}}^{AS}=-2m\varepsilon_{\mu_{1}}^{\text{\hskip 7.97224pt}\nu_{1}}(2\bar{J}_{2\alpha_{1}\nu_{1}}+q_{\nu_{1}}J_{2\alpha_{1}})=T_{\mu_{1};\alpha_{1}}^{SA}.

We have seen in this chapter that terms that may violate the RAGFs are local
polynomials in
P
and
q
momenta. These violating terms have values
determined from the set (
7.107
), (
7.126
), and (
7.127
). We
will see that choosing to save the linearity of integration operation,
manifested in the satisfaction of RAGF, will force us to establish finite
values for surface terms present in amplitudes. From now on, we will analyze
the results’ consequences and their implications for Einstein and Weyl
anomalies.

Chapter 8
Gravitational Anomalies

This chapter will list the formulas and general results developed in the
previous chapter as a form of organization. They are used in the sequence to
track the possible violating terms of the RAGFs that appear when we combine
the core elements in the permutations contributing to the full two-point
functions of the energy-momentum tensor. As we will adopt the following set
of indices
\left\langle T_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}\left(x\right)T_{\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}}\left(0\right)\right\rangle
to the energy-momentum
tensors in the correlator, see Eq. (
7.48
), we will have

T_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}}^{G}=-\frac{i}{64}\{[\mathcal{T}_{\mu_{12}\alpha_{12}}^{V}]+[\mathcal{T}_{\mu_{12}\alpha_{12}}^{A}]\}.

(8.1)

Hence, the formulas from the previous deductions have indices for even and
odd amplitudes arranged according to the sequence below

\displaystyle\hat{T}_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}}^{V}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1}\mu_{2}\alpha_{2}}^{VV}+\mathcal{T}_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1}\mu_{2}\alpha_{2}}^{AA}

\displaystyle\hat{T}_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}}^{A}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1}\mu_{2}\alpha_{2}}^{AV}+\mathcal{T}_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1}\mu_{2}\alpha_{2}}^{VA}.

The sum of permutations
\mu_{1}\leftrightarrow\mu_{2}
and from the
result
\alpha_{1}\leftrightarrow\alpha_{2}
deliver the vector and axial
part of the gravitational amplitude.

\displaystyle[\mathcal{T}_{\mu_{12}\alpha_{12}}^{V}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[\hat{T}_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}}^{V}]+[\hat{T}_{\mu_{2}\mu_{1}\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}}^{V}]+[\hat{T}_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\alpha_{2}\alpha_{1}}^{V}]+[\hat{T}_{\mu_{2}\mu_{1}\alpha_{2}\alpha_{1}}^{V}]

(8.2)

\displaystyle[\mathcal{T}_{\mu_{12}\alpha_{12}}^{A}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[\hat{T}_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}}^{A}]+[\hat{T}_{\mu_{2}\mu_{1}\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}}^{A}]+[\hat{T}_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\alpha_{2}\alpha_{1}}^{A}]+[\hat{T}_{\mu_{2}\mu_{1}\alpha_{2}\alpha_{1}}^{A}].

(8.3)

Basic Permutations
\mathcal{T}_{\mu\alpha\sigma\rho}^{\Gamma_{12}}
: As elaborated at the beginning of the previous chapter, the next
task after computing all the equations satisfied to the amplitudes is to
explore the basic permutations. Through their definition, we expanded our
definitions for derivative amplitudes accordingly. We have

\mathcal{T}_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1}\mu_{2}\alpha_{2}}^{\Gamma_{1}\Gamma_{2}}=4T_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1};\mu_{2}\alpha_{2}}^{\Gamma_{1}\Gamma_{2}}+2q_{\mu_{2}}T_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1};\alpha_{2}}^{\Gamma_{1}\Gamma_{2}}+q_{\alpha_{2}}(2T_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1};\mu_{2}}^{\Gamma_{1}\Gamma_{2}}+q_{\mu_{2}}T_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1}}^{\Gamma_{1}\Gamma_{2}}).

(8.4)

We must call attention to two features of the notation: The placement of
indices in
\hat{T}_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}}^{V}
is chosen
to mirror the ones from
T_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}}^{G}
,
however in
\mathcal{T}_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1}\mu_{2}\alpha_{2}}^{VV}
the
disposition emphasizes that the last two indices correspond to derivative
type. This attitude is helpful in the calculations to distinguish their
origin, either as the matrix or derivative indices. Another point in the
calligraphic letter
\mathcal{T}_{\mu\alpha\sigma\rho}^{\Gamma_{12}}

is to contrast the 4th-rank derivative amplitude that comes with a
semi-colon and the basic permutation involves four terms.

The basic permutations regarding derivatives structures were listed in (
7.42
)-(
7.45
). We resume them with the indexes:

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1}\mu_{2}\alpha_{2}}^{VV}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2(2T_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1};\mu_{2}\alpha_{2}}^{VV}+q_{\mu_{2}}T_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1};\alpha_{2}}^{VV})+q_{\alpha_{2}}(2T_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1};\mu_{2}}^{VV}+q_{\mu_{2}}T_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1}}^{VV})

(8.5)

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1}\mu_{2}\alpha_{2}}^{AA}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2(2T_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1};\mu_{2}\alpha_{2}}^{AA}+q_{\mu_{2}}T_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1};\alpha_{2}}^{AA})+q_{\alpha_{2}}(2T_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1};\mu_{2}}^{AA}+q_{\mu_{2}}T_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1}}^{AA})

(8.6)

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1}\mu_{2}\alpha_{2}}^{AV}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2(2T_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1};\mu_{2}\alpha_{2}}^{AV}+q_{\mu_{2}}T_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1};\alpha_{2}}^{AV})+q_{\alpha_{2}}(2T_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1};\mu_{2}}^{AV}+q_{\mu_{2}}T_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1}}^{AV})

(8.7)

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1}\mu_{2}\alpha_{2}}^{VA}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2(2T_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1};\mu_{2}\alpha_{2}}^{VA}+q_{\mu_{2}}T_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1};\alpha_{2}}^{VA})+q_{\alpha_{2}}(2T_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1};\mu_{2}}^{VA}+q_{\mu_{2}}T_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1}}^{VA}).

(8.8)

In the RAGFs, combinations of the basic derivative amplitudes from momenta
and metric contractions often arise:

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{\alpha_{1};\alpha_{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}k}{\left(2\pi\right)^{2}}\left(K_{1}+K_{2}\right)_{\alpha_{2}}\left[t_{\alpha_{1}}^{V}\left(k_{1}\right)+t_{\alpha_{1}}^{V}\left(k_{2}\right)\right]

(8.9)

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{\left(-\right)\alpha_{1};\mu_{2}\alpha_{2}}^{\Gamma_{1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{2}k}{\left(2\pi\right)^{2}}\left(K_{1}+K_{2}\right)_{\mu_{2}}\left(K_{1}+K_{2}\right)_{\alpha_{2}}[t_{\alpha_{1}}^{\Gamma_{1}}\left(k_{1}\right)-t_{\alpha_{1}}^{\Gamma_{1}}\left(k_{2}\right)],

(8.10)

where
\Gamma_{1}=\left\{V,A\right\}
. By projecting
K_{2}=K_{1}+q,
we
may decompose them in

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{\alpha_{1};\alpha_{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2T_{\left(+\right)\alpha_{1};\alpha_{2}}^{V}+q_{\alpha_{2}}T_{\left(+\right)\alpha_{1}}^{V}

(8.11)

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{\left(-\right)\alpha;\rho\sigma}^{\Gamma_{1}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 4T_{\left(-\right)\alpha;\rho\sigma}^{\Gamma_{1}}+2q_{\sigma}T_{\left(-\right)\alpha;\rho}^{\Gamma_{1}}+2q_{\rho}T_{\left(-\right)\alpha;\sigma}^{\Gamma_{1}}+q_{\rho}q_{\sigma}T_{\left(-\right)\alpha}^{\Gamma_{1}},

(8.12)

being careful to remind that
T_{\left(\pm\right)}^{\Gamma_{1}}
stands
for the difference or sum of one-point functions

T_{\left(\pm\right)}^{\Gamma_{1}}=T^{\Gamma_{1}}\left(k_{1}\right)\pm T^{\Gamma_{1}}\left(k_{2}\right).

For contractions with metric, only
\mathcal{B}_{\alpha_{1};\alpha_{2}}

arises; for momentum contraction in matrix indices, only
\mathcal{S}_{\left(-\right)\alpha_{1};\mu_{2}\alpha_{2}}^{\Gamma_{1}}
is
present. In contrast, for derivatives indexes, there arises both.

In the course of the previous chapter, we dealt with a set of finite

\displaystyle T_{\mu}^{SV}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+2m\left(2J_{2\mu}+q_{\mu}J_{2}\right)\equiv 0

(8.13)

\displaystyle T_{\mu}^{SA}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2m\varepsilon_{\mu\nu}\left(2J_{2}^{\nu}+q^{\nu}J_{2}\right)\equiv 0

(8.14)

\displaystyle 2T_{\mu;\alpha}^{PV}+q_{\alpha}T_{\mu}^{PV}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2m\varepsilon_{\mu\nu}q^{\nu}\left(2J_{2\alpha}+q_{\alpha}J_{2}\right)\equiv 0

(8.15)

\displaystyle 2T_{\mu;\alpha}^{PA}+q_{\alpha}T_{\mu}^{PA}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle+2mq_{\mu}\left(2J_{2\alpha}+q_{\alpha}J_{2}\right)\equiv 0

(8.16)

Amplitudes with non-negative power counting that we meet by studying the RHS
of RAGFs are combinations of the set
\left\{SV,SA,PV,PA\right\}
and
contain one or two derivative indices. Among those amplitudes is a set of

\displaystyle T_{\mu_{2};\alpha_{2}}^{SV}

(8.18)

\displaystyle T_{\mu_{1};\mu_{2}}^{AS}

\displaystyle=

(8.19)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\varepsilon_{\mu_{2}}^{\hskip 8.19447pt\nu}T_{\nu;\alpha_{2}}^{VS}

with attention to their finite parts.

To express the relations due to contractions with derivative indices we list

\displaystyle 2T_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1};\mu_{2}}^{VV}+q_{\mu_{2}}T_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1}}^{VV}

\displaystyle+g_{\mu_{2}\alpha_{1}}T_{\left(+\right)\mu_{1};\alpha_{2}}^{V}-g_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1}}T_{\left(+\right)\mu_{2};\alpha_{2}}^{V}.

8.1.1
Even amplitudes:
(\mathcal{T}_{\mu\alpha\sigma\rho}^{VV})
and
(\mathcal{T}_{\mu\alpha\sigma\rho}^{AA})

From now on, we will systematically explore all the results from the
amplitude combinations that effectively appear in the relations for the
gravitational amplitude. Starting by (
7.78
)-(
7.80
) follows

\mathcal{T}_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1}\mu_{2}\alpha_{2}}^{AA}=\mathcal{T}_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1}\mu_{2}\alpha_{2}}^{VV}-2mg_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1}}(4\bar{J}_{2\mu_{2}\alpha_{2}}+2q_{\alpha_{2}}J_{2\mu_{2}}),

(8.43)

The terms corresponding to the
J
-vector and
J
-scalar functions do not
appear because their combination is null. The relation is given by

\mathcal{T}_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1}\mu_{2}\alpha_{2}}^{AA}=\mathcal{T}_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1}\mu_{2}\alpha_{2}}^{VV}-4mg_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1}}T_{\mu_{2};\alpha_{2}}^{SV}.

(8.44)

That amounts to replacing double axial structures for the double vector
diminishing the number of operations necessary to express the relevant
results.

Internal Contractions

The contractions with internal indices for these amplitudes follow from the
definition

q^{\mu_{1}}\mathcal{T}_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1}\mu_{2}\alpha_{2}}^{VV}=2q^{\mu_{1}}(2T_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1};\mu_{2}\alpha_{2}}^{VV}+q_{\mu_{2}}T_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1};\alpha_{2}}^{VV})+q^{\mu_{1}}(2q_{\alpha_{2}}T_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1};\mu_{2}}^{VV}+q_{\alpha_{2}}q_{\mu_{2}}T_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1}}^{VV}).

(8.45)

The index of
q^{\mu_{1}}
hits only the matrix vertex of the amplitude,
and the consequence is that only the difference of one-point functions
appears, see (
7.129
), (
7.146
) and (
7.147
). Hence,
employing our definition

\mathcal{S}_{\left(-\right)\alpha_{1};\alpha_{2}\mu_{2}}^{V}=2[2T_{\left(-\right)\alpha_{1};\mu_{2}\alpha_{2}}^{V}+q_{\mu_{2}}T_{\left(-\right)\alpha_{1};\alpha_{2}}^{V}]+q_{\alpha_{2}}[2T_{\left(-\right)\alpha_{1};\mu_{2}}^{V}+q_{\alpha_{2}}T_{\left(-\right)\alpha_{1}}^{V}],

(8.46)

the equation obtained reads

q^{\mu_{1}}\mathcal{T}_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1}\mu_{2}\alpha_{2}}^{VV}=\mathcal{S}_{\left(-\right)\alpha_{1};\alpha_{2}\mu_{2}}^{V}=\mathcal{S}_{\alpha_{1};\mu_{2}\alpha_{2}}^{V}\left(k_{1}\right)-\mathcal{S}_{\alpha_{1};\mu_{2}\alpha_{2}}^{V}\left(k_{2}\right).

(8.47)

Note the symmetry in the indices corresponding to derivatives,
\mathcal{S}_{\alpha_{1};\mu_{2}\alpha_{2}}^{\Gamma_{1}}=\mathcal{S}_{\alpha_{1};\alpha_{2}\mu_{2}}^{\Gamma_{1}}
.

For the
\mathcal{T}^{AA}
, we could either use for its contraction the
PA
’s as in (
7.198
)-(
7.200
),

q^{\mu_{1}}\mathcal{T}_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1}\mu_{2}\alpha_{2}}^{AA}=-2m[4T_{\alpha_{1};\mu_{2}\alpha_{2}}^{PA}+2q_{\mu_{2}}T_{\alpha_{1};\alpha_{2}}^{PA}+q_{\alpha_{2}}(2T_{\alpha_{1};\mu_{2}}^{PA}+q_{\mu_{2}}T_{\alpha_{1}}^{PA})]+\mathcal{S}_{\left(-\right)\alpha_{1};\mu_{2}\alpha_{2}}^{V}

(8.48)

which is their composition of RAGFs. Using the connection with
\mathcal{T}^{VV}
(
8.44
), we have

q^{\mu_{1}}\mathcal{T}_{\mu_{1}\alpha_{1}\mu_{2}\alpha_{2}}^{AA}=\mathcal{S}_{\left(-\right)\alpha_{1};\mu_{2}\alpha_{2}}^{V}-4mq_{\alpha_{1}}T_{\mu_{2};\alpha_{2}}^{SV}.

(8.49)

The
PA
amplitudes did not appear since they are related to derivative
SV

through (
8.16
) and (
8.21
). In this amplitude, if the
operation is done in
\alpha_{1},
the RHS shows
AP
-structures, however,
with the opposite sign. As
T_{\mu}^{AP}=-T_{\mu}^{PA}
and so on for more
indices, hence the results written in terms of
T_{\mu_{2};\alpha_{2}}^{SV}
amplitude have the same functional form.

External Contractions

Terms from relations involving the derivative indices organize in the tensor

\mathcal{B}_{\mu;\alpha}
besides
